# Trial notes

Running observations from acceptance trials on the isolated Normal Survival server (`.test-acceptance`, port 25579), with the opportunities they point at. Newest first. Each trial's full log is under `artifacts/<run>/events.jsonl`.

## arena · 2026-09-22 · combat practice on the fixture server · in progress

Seven of the dream run's deaths in one night were combat, and every lesson cost a ten-minute restock. `scripts/arena.js` turns a lesson into a minute: nine drills carved into the Nether of `.test-combat` (port 25574), each staging one encounter that actually killed the bot, running the real survival and mob-hunt code against it, and scoring what it cost. Three runs a drill; a death anywhere fails it; the medians are what the next change has to beat. `sh .test-combat/start.sh &` then `MC_PORT=25574 node scripts/arena.js`. A watching player is opped, put in spectator and snapped onto the bot at every drill.

The fixes, in the order the drills forced them: stand under a hovering target; the mob the bot came for is not the survival layer's emergency; judge combat readiness by the kit carried rather than what is in hand; meet a pack at a bunker door, bounded to forty-five seconds; fight a swarm in the open until hurt, because blazes hover out of reach of a doorway.

**Round two (2026-09-22 06:20 UTC), five runs each on the blaze drills.** Five runs, not three, because the three-run medians had been hiding a death.

| drill | deaths | drops | damage | strikes |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| blaze_single | 0 -> 0 | 3/5 -> 4/5 | 5.7 -> 0 | 3 -> 3 |
| blaze_pair | 1 -> 0 | 2/5 -> 3/5 | 34.1 -> 25.9 | 2 -> 6 |

A single blaze now costs nothing at all: three swings, no damage, five runs out of five. The pair stopped killing the bot.

13. *Fitness to fight was judged by the hand, twice over.* `canBegin` demanded a sword in hand, so drawing the bow or placing one block made the bot unfit, and unfit means a half-second doze in the open. Two blazes shot it through five rounds of that. The check now asks whether the armour is on and a weapon is carried, which is the same mistake already fixed once in the hunt's claim on its quarry: the lesson is that "in hand" is a property of the last action, not of readiness.
14. *A bow is not the answer to a blaze, at least not in a room.* Measured head to head with the same fight: 34.7 damage with a bow and 32 arrows against 34.1 without. The ladder should not spend night rungs on a bow for the Nether's sake. The drill `blaze_pair_bow` keeps the comparison runnable.
15. *A shield now answers anything in the air* (`src/projectile-guard.js`): face the nearest incoming projectile and block while it crosses. Principled and cheap, but it made no measurable difference to the blaze pair on its own, because two shooters at different angles cannot both be inside one shield arc. Unmeasured against skeletons, which is where it should pay.
16. *Recovering in the open is not recovering.* With a shooter in view and health under eighteen, the hunt now digs in rather than dozing where it stands.
17. *The harness scored killed mobs as uncleared.* A hunt drill stops the moment the drop is picked up and the corpse can linger a tick in the entity list, so five runs that each killed their blaze read "cleared 1/5". Fixed to count confirmed kills.

**The tunnel mouth, tried and measured (2026-09-22 07:40 UTC), five runs each.** The idea was sound and the result was not: two shooters at different angles cannot both sit inside one shield arc, so meet them at a doorway where they have to arrive one at a time.

| blaze_pair, same fight, three policies | deaths | drops | damage |
|---|---|---|---|
| fight in the open | 0 | 3/5 | 25.9 |
| always go and find a wall | 1 | 1/5 | 40.7 |
| use a wall only when one is at hand | 1 | 2/5 | 28.5 |

18. *A doorway is worth having, not worth travelling for.* Sent to find a wall nine blocks off, the bot took forty-one damage crossing the room under fire and arrived with nothing; fighting where it stood cost twenty-six. The bunker now needs a wall within five blocks (`nearWall`), which is the difference between using cover and going shopping for it.
19. *The bunker is safety, not a harvest.* With a wall three blocks away the swarm drill takes **zero** damage and collects **zero** rods, five runs out of five: blazes hover outside the mouth and will not come to the sword. The rods come from single blazes, which now cost nothing at all, so the policy is to survive the crowd and farm the stragglers.
20. *The wall search was finding the floor.* Every block of a netherrack floor is natural rock and every one is nearer than any wall, so an unfiltered search returned hundreds of floor tiles and no wall: the bot stood in the open being shot and reported that there was no rock to dig into, in a room made of rock. Candidates are now taken at the bot's own level.
21. *A straight shaft is a shooting gallery.* The bunker digs a turn at its end where the rock allows, so nothing outside has a line to the back. Implemented and unit-tested, but not yet demonstrated in play: the corner was never reached in a measured run, because the runs that got that far were the ones already going badly.
22. *A swallowed failure reads as a bad decision.* `digBunker` threw "no rock here" into an empty catch for two whole rounds of measurement, so from outside the bot merely looked like it was choosing to stand in the open. Every bunker failure is now recorded on the hunt and reported by the arena.

**Full set (2026-09-22 09:10 UTC), ten drills, five runs each, forty-five runs.** One death, against three in twenty-seven baseline runs, and it was not a mob.

| drill | verdict | deaths | damage | secs |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| zombie_single | PASS -> PASS | 0 -> 0 | 1.5 -> 1.5 | 7.5 -> 7.5 |
| wither_skeleton_single | PASS -> PASS | 0 -> 0 | 1.9 -> 1.9 | 5.4 -> 5.5 |
| wither_skeleton_pair | PASS -> PASS | 0 -> 0 | 15.4 -> 0 | 18.1 -> 33.8 |
| hoglin_single | PASS -> FAIL | 0 -> 0 | 8.8 -> 11.2 | 8.6 -> 9.4 |
| hoglin_herd | FAIL -> PASS | 3 -> 0 | 34.8 -> 0 | survives, does not clear |
| blaze_single | FAIL -> PASS | 0 -> 0 | never fought -> 1.0 | 7.2, 5/5 killed, 3 rods |
| blaze_pair | FAIL -> FAIL | 0 -> 0 | never fought -> 32.2 | 77.9, 1/5 |
| blaze_swarm_wall | FAIL -> FAIL | 0 -> 0 | never fought -> 0 | survives, no rods |

The baseline zeros mean "never fought", not "took no damage": those drills ended without a swing.

23. *The only death in forty-five runs was a fall.* `fell from a high place`, twenty damage, immediately after killing its blaze. Following a flying thing means towering up to it and a tower is a place to fall off. Something overhead is now reached by standing under it, and the drill went from 6.7 damage with a death to **1.0 damage, five kills in five runs, three rods, seven seconds** — the single largest step of the whole exercise, from a fix aimed at something else.
24. *A mob that will not engage makes a drill measure nothing.* Two runs in five of each single-mob drill had the mob wander instead of attacking; the nudge that brings it to three blocks fired once and then gave up. It now repeats every eight seconds, and the singles went from three clears in five to five and four.

**The blaze pair, worked on directly (2026-09-22 10:30 UTC), five runs a step.**

| blaze_pair | deaths | rods | damage |
|---|---|---|---|
| baseline: sealed itself in, never fought | 0 | 0 | never fought |
| fighting, before this round | 0 | 0 | 32.2 |
| after the fire rule | 0 | 3 | 22.8 |
| after building cover | 0 | 4 | 16.1 |

25. *The bot would not fight while it was on fire.* `canBegin` refused any fight while the on-fire metadata bit was set, which against a blaze is the normal state of the fight: the fireball that lights the bot is thrown by the thing it came to kill, and there is no water in the Nether to put it out. So the first hit ended the fight, and the bot stood in the open, burning, being shot by the blaze it had just declined to attack. One blaze never lit it in seven seconds and cost a single point of damage; two lit it immediately and cost thirty. Fire now ends a fight only under ten health, where the fire really is what is killing it. This one rule took the pair from 32.2 damage and no rods to 22.8 and three.
26. *Build the doorway where you stand.* Below the fight floor with shooters in view and no wall within five blocks, the bot now places two blocks one step toward them and breaks the line, instead of waiting in the open for health that cannot come back while it is being shot. Two blocks placed beat nine blocks walked: 22.8 damage to 16.1, and four rods in five runs. The best two runs cost 6.7 and 9.4.
27. *Read the trace before changing anything.* Every wrong guess this session (the bow, the tunnel mouth for an open-room pair) came from reasoning about the fight; both real fixes came from printing the bot's own health and action, second by second, and noticing it quit at eighteen health with full food and full kit. The number that mattered was the one that should not have been there.

**Back on the dream run (2026-09-22 11:20 UTC).** Jev resumed at the fortress with every arena fix loaded, and immediately showed three blockers the arena had never staged, because the arena had no spawner room under a roof in a forest full of hoglins.

28. *A goal at your own height is one you are already standing on.* Perched on the fortress roof with the blazes below, the only approach offered was level with the bot, so the route "succeeded" without moving and no fight ever began. The fall fix had taught the code about targets overhead and left the mirror case wrong. Something below is reached by going down to it.
29. *A hoglin twelve blocks away vetoed every blaze.* The isolation rule gave any other species a twelve-block berth, and the crimson forest above a fortress is full of hoglins, so no blaze was ever isolated enough to fight. Shooters keep the wide berth; everything else now has to be within eight.
30. *My own watchdog was the third blocker.* The fifteen-second "watching and getting nowhere" rule set the mob aside, and in a room with twelve blazes it set one aside every fifteen seconds until it had avoided all of them and had nothing left to hunt. It now changes the situation instead, building cover so they must come round it, and only gives up on a target after three tries. A stall-breaker that removes the work is not a stall-breaker.
31. *The spawner room is a drill now* (`blaze_spawner`, arena `tower`): four blazes in a sealed chamber under the floor the bot stands on. It reproduces the live wall exactly, and the hunt now digs down through the roof to reach them. Three runs: one death, one run where nothing happened, and one where all four died for **zero damage** through cover and a tunnel. Inconsistent, which is what a drill is for; the rods still went uncollected in the chamber below, which is the next thing.

**The spawner room, second pass (2026-09-22 12:15 UTC), three runs each.** Both drills pass, and the arena now counts only kills the bot caused.

| drill | deaths | bot kills | rods | damage |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| blaze_spawner | 1 -> 0 | 0 -> 2 | 0 -> 1 | 10.7 -> 9.4 |
| blaze_swarm_open | 0 -> 0 | 0 -> 5 | 0 -> 3 | - -> 14.1 |

32. *A crash on every hurt tick.* The recovery branch referenced `state` two hundred lines before `const state` was declared: a temporal-dead-zone error, thrown out of the hunt on every tick the bot was below eighteen health with a shooter in view. It read, from outside, as a bot that simply did nothing when hurt.
33. *The exemption had a hole in it.* The hunt's claim on its quarry stopped the survival layer sealing against distant blazes, and also stopped it swinging at near ones: four blazes sat at two blocks while the deliberate fight found no candidate and the reactive one had been told to look away. Forty-three damage, no swing thrown. A mob within a sword's reach is never exempt; the emergency answer to a blaze in the face is the swing, whatever the hunt intended.
34. *Three of a kind in view is not "too many" for a bot at full health.* The isolation rule refused every candidate at a spawner, where three in the air is the floor, and in the arena the same swarm fought at full health came home with rods while watched it came home with nothing. The crowd stops a fight only once the bot is already hurt. The bunker also digs fortress brick now, which is the only rock a fortress has.
35. *Blazes die on their own in a sealed room* (one of three in the spawner drill), against the walls. Counting those as kills sent a rod-collection fix down a blind alley; the arena now credits a kill only if the bot struck that mob within six seconds.

Live, the same code has dug Jev down from the roof at y=83 to y=79, four blocks from the blazes' level, and it is still stalking; no rod yet.

**Into the spawner room, live (2026-09-22 17:00 UTC).** Twelve blockers between Jev on the fortress roof and a blaze in reach, found one at a time by reading the live trace, each fixed and tested. The first live blaze fight at the spawner happened at 17:01.

36. *A one-block hole is not a fight.* The descent left the bot level with the blazes in a hole one block high; the ceiling blocked every line of sight, so nothing was a candidate. A shaft is now cut toward them a step a tick, two high.
37. *The shaft's own safety rule pushed it backwards.* A travelling shaft may not step toward a mob, and its fallback picks whichever way is furthest from one: the target pulled forward, the rule shoved back. A shaft dug *at* a mob (`approach`) ignores that rule.
38. *The anti-loop penalty walked it in circles.* Sixteen blocks per revisit, meant to stop a travelling shaft looping; after a few hundred steps in one pocket the least-visited cell won whatever its distance, and the bot paced 19, 20, 21, 19 for an hour. An approach is scored by distance alone.
39. *It built cover in its own tunnel.* The fifteen-second "break their line" placed two blocks one step toward the blazes, which was the next cell the shaft meant to dig. No cover while a shaft is being dug at them.
40. *The pathfinder refused every step toward a blaze.* Every neighbour is filtered through "safe from hostiles", and closer to a shooter is unsafe by definition, so the walk into each freshly dug cell failed without an error. The mob the hunt has claimed is not avoided by the pathfinder while the bot is fit. This is the change that got it into the room.
41. *A crash on every hurt tick* (`state` used before its declaration) and *an exemption hole* (four blazes at two blocks, nobody swinging): a mob within a sword's reach is never exempt from the emergency swing.
42. *Rotten flesh is a last resort.* Sealed in at ten health and seventeen hunger with two rotten flesh in the pack, it healed nothing for ten minutes because the flesh was on the unsafe list. Its Hunger effect costs under a point; it gives four. Spider eyes and pufferfish stay banned.
43. *A fit bot does not hide from its quarry, and the quarry does not block the door.* Beside a spawner the watchers never leave and no exit is ever twenty blocks from a blaze, so the pocket held it for twenty minutes, first waiting and then, once it decided to leave, refusing every door. Both checks now ignore the claimed mob. The wait also says who is watching now: the reason the second check was found in one look after an hour of guessing.
44. *The Nether end of a portal was never remembered.* Only the side it was standing beside when leaving got recorded, so a hungry bot 120 blocks away did not know where the way out was. Any portal in sight is now remembered on the side the bot is on.
45. *Death nine: a fight that could only go one way.* It left the pocket at fifteen health and seventeen hunger, no food, and took on the spawner; with regeneration off every point spent was gone. A hostile fight now starts only if the health spent can come back: hunger eighteen or food in the pack. Otherwise the fight is the walk home.
46. *The kit-restore aid had been blind to worn armour since the first scratch.* A damaged item carries a nested `components` block and the parser stopped at its first closing brace, so after the armour took damage the aid saw only the shield, and this death lost the iron set. Fixed and verified against the server's real output; the set was put back by console, labelled here as a controlled-run intervention.

**After the workflows audit (2026-09-22 18:30 UTC), eleven drills, three runs each, thirty-three runs.** A read-only audit from another session traced the combat rules against each other rather than against one scenario each; these are the fixes that held up when checked against the code. No deaths anywhere.

| drill | deaths | cleared | rods | damage | before |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| zombie_single | 0 | 3/3 | - | 0 | 1.5 |
| wither_skeleton_single | 0 | 3/3 | - | 0 | 1.9 |
| wither_skeleton_pair | 0 | 3/3 | - | 0 | 0 |
| hoglin_single | 0 | 3/3 | - | 10.3 (FAIL, bar 10) | 8.8 / 5.6 / 13.0 |
| hoglin_herd | 0 | survives | - | 0 | 0 |
| blaze_single | 0 | 3/3 | 2 | 5.7 | 1.0 |
| blaze_pair | 0 | 2/3 | 3 | **6.7 (PASS)** | 16 to 32 |
| blaze_pair_bow | 0 | 3/3 | 1 | 0 | - |
| blaze_spawner | 0 | 0/3 | 3 | 13.8 | 9.4, 1 rod |
| blaze_swarm_wall | 0 | 1/3 | 2 | 9.2 | 0, no rods ever |
| blaze_swarm_open | 0 | 0/3 | 2 | 17.4 | 14.1, 3 rods |

The pair passes for the first time, and the wall swarm brings rods home for the first time. `hoglin_single` is the noisy drill of note 9, not a regression aimed at; `blaze_single` moved from 1.0 to 5.7 on three runs, which is under the five-point noise line but worth watching.

47. *Burning made every hunt decision stale.* Fresh meant health exactly equal to the snapshot, and a bot on fire loses health every second: every answer came back stale and the bot stood in the open asking again. A few points lost while Jev answers is still the same fight. With Jev unreachable, the nearest checked candidate is fought instead of throwing.
48. *Another blaze beside a blaze made it unfightable.* The sweep rule (no other mob within four blocks of the target) counted kin, so no blaze at a spawner ever qualified, whatever the crowd rule above it said. Kin are the crowd rule's business; other kinds still need the room.
49. *One shooter list, not three.* They disagreed about the witch and the crossbow piglin. Also one fight floor (fourteen) read by the hunt, the danger layer and the drop down to a fight.
50. *Zombified piglins were never a threat, and fire ended the piglin truce.* The damage packet names who hit the bot. A zombified piglin or piglin that hits it turns that whole group hostile for thirty seconds; a blaze's fire no longer ends the gold truce and starts a second fight.
51. *No footing to flee to in the Nether.* The escape searched only overworld ground, so every retreat and the step back from a lava edge found nothing and fell through to sealing in.
52. *A drop to a fight arrived too hurt to fight.* Nine blocks from sixteen health landed at ten, under the floor, and the claim on the quarry lapsed on landing. The drop now keeps the fight floor, and never lands beside lava.
53. *Cover and door took turns on the same cell.* Cover the hunt raised is kept for a minute, and a bunker hold that ends with nothing is not re-entered for two.
54. *A lone mob just outside the sword was walled off.* Between reach (3.0) and the charge's minimum (3.2) nothing swung and nothing charged. A single mob that does not shoot is now charged before any wall, and the step toward a hovering blaze is taken only onto solid, lava-free ground.

**Standing still is a bug (2026-09-22 19:40 UTC).** Every stall found today had one shape: a step returned having done nothing, or a wait was chosen with nothing else on offer, and the next tick landed in the same place. A harvest chore failing silently twenty times a second; a sleep refused for a block on the bed, then a night behind a wall; a Nether food gate with the chest lid blocked and the animal search resting, spinning at twenty passes a second. So the rule is now structural rather than a patch per case.

55. *Stillness is checked, not hoped against* (`src/stillness.js`). Activity is what the world can see: a block moved, a dig, a pickup, a blow landed, the pockets changed. Twenty seconds without any, outside a short list of waits worth making (asleep, a fight, holding a door), is a stall. It is logged with what the bot was on, and both the work loop and the idle loop hand it a detour: work that can be done from here (the idle chores, ore in reach, a look around, the night mine), Jev's pick, three minutes each. A detour that fails rests for five minutes; a rung that stalls three times in ten minutes waits its turn. The idle loop's "rest" option is gone.
56. *The night is mined, not waited out.* With nothing watching the pocket and no bed to sleep in, the bot digs a mine from the shelter: toward ore in view of the rock, or down to a working depth and along a branch, one staircase step a tick. A tunnel in rock is as closed as the pocket. A mob that shows is the survival layer's; a usable bed comes first; at dawn the mine is left for the day's work.
57. *The Nether food gate always acts.* The chest, then the plot, then the hunt, with a search that had been set aside taken up again; after twenty working minutes it lets the crossing go with what there is.
58. *The base is not a place to leave a pocket standing.* A pocket at the base comes down when it is left in the morning; a block on the stash chest's lid or on the bed is dug off before opening or sleeping; a sleep is judged by the clock even when mineflayer times out waiting for its event, and a refusal the server names is logged.
59. *Measured.* Seconds still, per hour and per reason, are kept with the survival state; `node scripts/stillness-report.js` prints the table.

**First hour on the stillness rule (2026-09-22 19:35 to 19:55 UTC).** Two stalls in twenty minutes, 40 seconds still in all, each answered within the second: waiting for bedtime became a night mine that took six coal from under the base, and a hunt standing "recovering" at full health on the fortress roof became a look around. The bed slept on the first try after the block on it was dug off.

60. *Recovery was the answer to every failed fight check, including footing.* At 20 health and 18 food on a fortress roof the hunt stood "recovering": the check it failed was dry standing ground, and waiting never changes that. Recovery is now for health and hunger only; bad footing falls through to the stalk, which moves.
61. *Death ten: it walked through its own portal and off the platform.* Heading home for food, it reached the Nether portal, faced it and held forward for eight seconds waiting to be taken. A portal takes a player who stands in it for about four seconds; at walking speed the bot crossed the one-block sheet in a fraction of a second and kept walking in a straight line (x fixed at -54.5, z from -11 through the portal at -16 to -29), off the platform's edge thirteen blocks beyond, twenty-four blocks down. The server's rejoin position gave the death point; the steps logged before it gave the line. It now steps in sneaking (a sneaking player cannot walk off an edge), stops the moment its feet are in the sheet, and stands there. The same routine is used going in, so the Overworld side had the same flaw. The kit-restore aid put the kit back (controlled-run intervention, not acceptance evidence).

62. *Death eleven, and the falls behind it: code that moves the bot by holding a key.* A second fall came twenty minutes after the portal fix: on the fortress roof at full health, six blocks sideways and twenty-three down in two seconds. The pathfinder cannot do that (drops of three at most, no parkour), so the suspects were the places that hold a movement key directly, and the user had watched one of them do it: the bridge. It sneaked only while stepping onto each new block, and stood upright at the end of a one-block span over the drop while it dug ahead, turned and placed, under blaze fire. The bridge now crouches from the first block to the last and will not be laid with a shooter that can see the bot. Two more keys held blind were closed at the same time: the step into a hole held forward for two and a half seconds whether or not the hole was found (now: forward until over it, then drop or give up), and the walk straight at a drop now crouches unless the drop is above.

63. *Death twelve: drowned in its own night mine, with the process frozen.* The night mine had taken twenty ores when it met a copper it could not reach ("no dry route away from the blocked staircase"). Two faults compounded. The mine forgot a failed target but not why, so the nearest ore was the same one and it turned eighty times in place; and the detour that runs the mine looped `while (await nightMine())` with no pause, while a failed step returned at once. A loop of promises that never waits on anything starves the event loop: the dashboard stopped answering, physics and the air check stopped, and the bot drowned where it stood. The loop now yields between steps and gives up after six failed ones, and an ore that fails three times is set aside for ten minutes. The same run also brought home its first three live blaze rods.

**Loops, structurally (2026-09-22 21:10 UTC).** Every loop of the day had one shape: steps that each succeeded while the goal got no closer, a safety filter that quietly removed the only useful step, a chooser with no memory of what had failed, a hand-held key with its own edge rule, or a loop of promises that never waited. Four pieces answer the shape rather than the cases.

64. *A flight recorder.* Every live frame is written to `.bot-state/flight`, with the keys held, whether the pathfinder is moving, and the name of the code holding the keys, sampled four times a second while keys are held outside the pathfinder. `node scripts/flight.js --deaths` prints each death with the ten seconds before it; three deaths today were reconstructed by inference because this did not exist.
65. *One way to hold keys.* `src/motion.js` `move()`: crouched by default, upright only with a stated reason, a stop condition every tick, five seconds at most, every key given back, the move named for the recorder. Ten hand-written loops now go through it, and tests fail on a movement key held anywhere else or an await loop that never sleeps.
66. *One progress measure and one memory of failure.* `src/progress.js`: progress is a new best, not a step that worked; failed attempts are remembered by action and target with a reason and a rest, per world. Four private "set aside" maps now use it. The staircase records why it ruled each closer cell out, and a dig at a fixed target (the night mine, the approach shaft) fails with that reason instead of stepping sideways.
67. *Terrain drills.* `scripts/terrain.js` stages the places behind the day's failures on the arena server and runs the real code: an ore in the wall of a flooded cave, a bridge over a drop, the same bridge under a blaze's eye, a portal on a platform over a drop, a stash chest with a block on its lid. All five pass twice; the flooded ore run against the code from before its fix fails, as it should.

| drill | runs | passed | deaths | falls |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| flooded_ore | 2 | 2 | 0 | 0 |
| bridge_over_drop | 2 | 2 | 0 | 0 |
| bridge_under_fire | 2 | 2 | 0 | 0 |
| portal_platform | 2 | 2 | 0 | 0 |
| chest_lid | 2 | 2 | 0 | 0 |

68. *Step three finished: every set-aside in one memory, every stall on one supervisor.* All the private "try again later" state moved into the per-world Attempts memory, each entry with its action, target, reason and rest: blocks set aside as unreachable, failed options, obsidian crust, hunt targets, the cover and bunker cooldowns, the bed route, the sleep retry, failed escape spots, the paused food search, deferred rungs, the wool-bed, village-bed and home-site searches, the stash chest, the boat crossing (now rested half an hour after two failures rather than retired for the goal) and the dream's failed launch. `watch()` is the one supervisor: an activity reports what it is after and how far off it is, and after no new best in a number of steps or a span of time the target is given up with the reason. The night mine's target, the hunt's stalking, the stock food search, the wool search and the Nether food gate all report to it; each used to keep its own clock, and several ran from the first look whatever progress came after.

69. *The flight recorder, third pass.* After rotation and slim heartbeats it still wrote forty megabytes in thirty-one minutes: three quarters of the frames were work steps at eleven kilobytes each, every one carrying the whole goal, because the goal's step counter changes on every step and "write it only when it changed" never skipped it. Step frames are now slim like the heartbeats, other frames carry a compact goal (request, step, survival action, last error), the whole goal goes once a minute and with every death or connection, and the decision view only with decision frames. Step frames went from 10,869 bytes to 536, and the file from about 78 MB an hour to 12.

70. *Eighty-eight rounds of one corridor.* On the way back to its Nether portal for food, ninety-six blocks off, the staircase walked a twenty-five-block fortress corridor from (18, 46) to (29, 23) and back, thirty-seven rounds in eleven minutes, eighty-eight in all. Each round that gained nothing reset its best to wherever it stood and cleared its map of walked cells, so the next round measured itself from scratch and walked the same cells as new. It was never still, so the stillness rule never saw it. Now a round keeps its best and its walked cells, three rounds with no new best set that staircase aside for ten minutes in the Attempts memory, and a portal more than forty-eight blocks off gets a thirty-two-block leg by the pathfinder (which bridges and climbs) before any digging. A leg that makes no ground rests two minutes.

71. *Death thirteen: a search with no origin.* The walked leg worked: from ninety-six blocks out Jev was back at its Nether portal within two minutes of the restart. It came through at 0.6 health with nothing to eat, and the Overworld food search threw "Cannot read properties of undefined (reading 'x')" every half second until something finished it by the portal. The cause was mine: persist turns each search's heading (the peer recheck's fix) by rebuilding it as `{ attempts, frontier }`, with no origin, and `explore` reads the origin before anything else, so every persist set up the next crash. `searchFor` now fills in a missing origin. And a TypeError, ReferenceError or RangeError now logs its stack once as `[bug]` and puts its top frames in `goal.lastErrorAt`, which the flight recorder keeps, so the next one says where it is.

72. *Full pockets: chickens killed and left lying.* After death thirteen Jev killed a dozen chickens in five minutes and picked up none, standing on the drops: every slot was taken. The kit restore (controlled-run aid) gave a full kit on top of the gear the bot had walked back and collected, so it carried two swords, two diamond pickaxes, three shields, two flint and steels and a spare helmet, leggings and boots, and the tidy rule only knew surplus stone. Now: when crowded with nothing over the stone caps, spare gear goes (the best of each tool plus one spare pickaxe stays; armour no better than what is worn goes); the hunt makes room before it kills and refuses with "no room in my pockets" if it cannot; the mine step and the night mine do the same before digging for drops. The kit restore now clears the death drops before giving the kit, so it no longer doubles the inventory.

73. *A pocket built and dug out of, with a bed in sight.* The only beds Jev knew were one carried (lost at the death; the kit had none) and its home bed four hundred blocks away, so at nightfall the choice was a sealed pocket, and the night mine then dug out of it, reseal and dig, four times in twenty seconds. A bed in view (Overworld, within 48 blocks, foot half, not occupied, a cell to stand in) is now a bed to sleep in, by the same walk, clear-above and sleep as the home bed. The reseal-and-dig churn itself is still open.

74. *Four hundred refused hunts.* The refusal worked and was its own loop: with the spares gone the pockets filled with things all under their caps (190 cobblestone, 104 coal, 67 netherrack, nether bricks, quartz), the tidy had nothing to drop, and 432 hunts in half an hour ended "No room in my pockets for mutton". Room for something wanted is now made from an expendable list (dirt, gravel, saplings, fence, netherrack down to 32, cobblestone down to 64, soul sand, nether bricks, seeds down to 8), the smallest stack first, one stack at a time, for the hunt, the mine step and the night mine.

75. *Two cells on the way up.* The climb out of a shallow pocket at (-1050, 66, 312) stepped between two cells for minutes, its climbing step blocked; the stillness rule fired three times and its detours did not move it. The three-round give-up (note 70) would have needed a hundred and forty-four steps, and could never have held: the way-up target is the nearest landing and moves a block with each step, while the give-up was keyed by the exact cell, and it was written on a copy of the goal that the ascent throws away. Now a round ends as soon as the step taken has been walked four times with no new best, give-ups are keyed by the eight-block area, the ascent shares the goal's memory, and a given-up ascent turns to another landing or, with none, a heading of its own that turns at each give-up.

76. *Pinned by two skeletons.* With the give-ups working, every way up from that pocket was given up in turn within a minute: two skeletons stood in the shade nine blocks up the slope at mid-morning, and every cell toward the surface was "closer to a shooter", with Jev at full health in iron and diamond. A travelling staircase with no gaining step because of a mob now claims the mob, the way the hunt claims its quarry, when Jev is fit to fight and the mobs are at most two of one kind and not creepers: the steps toward them open, and the defence layer swings at the first in reach. The claim lapses with fitness, as the hunt's does.

77. *It was never underground.* A spectator probe showed the "pocket" at (-1050, 66, 312) was Jev's own netherrack shelter on a hillside under a spruce, open air a block to the east. Sealed in, no walking route existed, so the ravine rule ("open sky is not an exit") set a minimum height of 70 from the higher ground above, and that requirement stuck: standing on open ground at 66 would not have counted. Everything after (the two-cell pacing, the skeletons, the given-up staircases) was the bot trying to reach y 70 up a hill. A given-up ascent now drops the minimum height. And the dusk churn is named: from that pocket the night mine's first step is into the open, the shelter plan fills it, and the mine digs it again every two seconds; a pocket mined out of and resealed three times in two minutes is now waited in for the night (the wait is a permitted one). (Later: the count was of tries, not dig-outs. With full pockets the night mine declined to start, three quiet tries made "three reseals", and at y 23 under solid rock Jev waited out the night in its pocket. Only a try that dug counts now.) Not unit-tested; checked on the live run.

78. *Death fourteen: fireballed while running back to the blaze.* A blaze hunt landed one swing and cost ten health in thirty seconds; the bot recovered to 15.7, stalked again, and fell back to the escape at 8.7. The escape scored its footing only against the mobs in view that instant: at 00:07:31 a blaze (ran west), at 00:07:35 a piglin twenty blocks off (ran east, eighteen blocks straight back past the blaze), dead at 00:07:44. The escape now gains ground on the nearest of every hostile within thirty-two blocks, seen or not, and keeps its route clear of all of them; the test fails on the old code. Two blazes remain the weakest fight (arena note 10).

79. *Patrolling its own bricks.* The fortress sweep stood still for 24 seconds with no detour to take, pacing (8, 50, 45) and (7, 50, 46): four hundred and fourteen legs begun, each one new, so none was ever twenty seconds old and the rule that turns the sweep never fired. The "fortress" in view was a few nether bricks beside it, its own: the kit carries sixty-four and they are building blocks for pockets and bridges. Any fortress block in view made the sweep "inside", with nothing fresh to walk it patrolled, and after six patrols it cleared its leg and began another. A fortress is now at least twenty-four fortress blocks in view. That was not all of it: logging the leg showed the target kept between calls and lost inside one. With the bricks in view and none twelve blocks off to walk to, the patrol cleared its walked list and returned having done nothing, six times, then dropped its leg and began another. Bricks with nothing to walk to are now shunned for ten minutes like an unapproachable face, and the leg in hand is walked; the test fails on the old code.

80. *Thirty blocks under its own portal.* On the way back for food, Jev ended up on the floor of a Nether cavern at y 36, beside a lava pool fed by a lava fall, two blocks sideways of its portal and thirty-one below it: the portal stands on the platform it built over the cavern. The staircase went ninety-eight rounds there (the three-round give-up never came, because the walk before each round nudged a new best), and the pathfinder, which may build towers, never finished a search for one that tall. A portal overhead (more than six up, within six sideways) is now climbed by a pillar first: jump, a block where the feet were, what is overhead dug; it stops at the height, at a block it must not dig, or with lava or water in or beside the next cells up, and the walk takes over from there. The first version sat in the walk to a remembered portal and never ran: with the portal loaded in view, the return goes enter, fail, staircase, by another road. It now sits in the one step every portal return ends in. Live, the pillar then stopped before its first block: the column it stood in had the lava fall beside it. It now walks first to the nearest column, within six blocks of the portal, clear of lava and water in and beside every cell up to the portal's height. Then two rules undid each other: the pillar climbed to 48, and the pillar descent (for a bot stranded on a column) took it back to 47, again and again. A column raised on purpose is now left standing for ten minutes.

81. *Death fifteen: held its position in lava.* Under the portal, at ten health with nothing to eat, a hoglin knocked Jev into the lava pool beside it at eight; the survival layer answered with "hold defensive position" four times, in the lava, until it burned. Nothing looked at whether the bot was in lava. Now it is the first question of every survival step: in lava, the nearest cell with a floor and air for the body, forward and jumping at it, before any fight, hold or decision.

82. *A furnace placed from the water.* Back at the base after death fifteen, the pond had run into the pit beside the furnace and table, and Jev, floating there, placed a new furnace and then could not reach it: "I can't reach the furnace I placed", then the table, and thirty seconds still. A workstation is now placed and used from dry ground: floating in water, the bot first steps to the nearest dry cell with air above (the lava exit's search, which now also refuses water and measures from cell centres, not corners).

83. *Death sixteen: back through the portal with three pieces of food.* After respawning at home the blaze hunt walked straight back through the portal: the food gate is a rung on the ladder, and the hunt's own way to the Nether did not pass it. Jev fought a blaze down to four health, recovered to eighteen, and a wither skeleton knocked it off the ledge by the portal, thirty blocks. The gate now also stands at the crossing itself, whoever asks for it: a Nether reserve (24 food points, or twenty working minutes of trying and at least some food), and sixteen health, waited for beside the portal (a permitted wait while healing).

84. *Death seventeen, and the ledge drills.* Healthy and fed this time, Jev was shot at by a skeleton thirteen blocks off on a Nether ledge, sealed in where it stood on the edge row, and went over, thirty blocks. Two terrain drills now stage it: `ledge_fight` (a wither skeleton coming at the bot on the edge of a three-wide ledge over a forty-block drop) and `ledge_shot` (a skeleton shooting across a gap). The first runs of both were worthless: the clearing fill was 49,000 blocks, over the server's 32,768 limit, so it did nothing and the bot was placed inside solid netherrack ("suffocated in a wall"); a clear now stays under the limit. On the old code `ledge_fight` fell once in three and `ledge_shot` held. With a mob within eight blocks and a drop or lava beside it, Jev now first steps to the nearest cell with floor on every side and no lava within two, before fighting, holding or sealing: `ledge_fight` 6 of 6, `ledge_shot` 6 of 6. Small samples; the live run is the rest of the evidence.

85. *Critical hits.* Every melee swing (defence, the mob hunt, the food hunt) now jumps and lands on the way down when it safely can: on the ground, out of liquid, head room, no drop beside it, not sprinting, and not at a creeper, hoglin or zoglin. Two things the first version got wrong, both found with the server's own crit animation: standing still reads a small downward velocity, so it swung the instant the feet left the ground; and the server reads the fall a tick behind, so the swing waits for two ticks of falling. Arena, five or eight runs each, plain against crit: zombie 3 hits in 5.2 s against 2 in 4.3 s; lone wither skeleton 3 hits in 3.9 s against 2 in 3.9 s (1.9 damage against 0); lone blaze 6.2 damage in 18.4 s against 4.2 in 7.2 s (eight each); hoglin 9.6 damage against 15.2, because a hoglin throws what it hits into the air and the jump made that worse, so hoglins and zoglins are struck plainly (9.2 after). Ledge drills still 3 of 3.

86. *Two findings from the decision atlas (peer audit).* (1) "Get food or carry on" went to Jev every five seconds at full health and hunger, about thirty times in two bursts (00:07 and 00:40), obtain_food at 0.96 to 0.98 each time, about 800 tokens a call; the recordings show a stock top-up with nothing carried (0 of 12 points, not the expedition reserve the audit read). A top-up once chosen is now held for five minutes, while food is still wanted and nothing needs shelter; which source is still Jev's to choose each time. The test fails on the old code. (2) Every hunt option said "chicken must be cooked before eating", a rabbit included. The text is now the animal's own: its food, and whether raw can poison or is only worth more cooked.

87. *Pearls from piglins.* The pearl rung was the enderman hunt alone (trading with clerics is possible, measured here: only an Expert cleric sells them, five emeralds each, and village clerics start at Novice). Measured on this server: 160 gold ingots thrown to eight penned piglins bought 18 ender pearls, about nine ingots a pearl, plus 13 obsidian, 21 crying obsidian, soul sand, quartz, string, and a lot of gravel and blackstone. With the user's choice of bartering alongside the hunt: in the Nether, short of pearls, with gold on hand and a piglin in view, the ladder now barters before going home. The step makes ingots from nuggets, wears a gold piece (carried golden boots, or a pair from four spare ingots), throws one ingot to each of up to three adult piglins, waits out their admiring, and picks up the pearls and the useful loot (obsidian, string, quartz, soul sand, fire charges), leaving the gravel. Nether gold ore within reach is mined by rule while pearls are short. The `barter` terrain drill: forty ingots thrown, two to four pearls, golden boots on, no piglin harmed. Away from the Nether the hunt goes on as before.

88. *No shore under a roof.* In the base's flooded pit again, Jev bobbed between "surface" and "wait for bedtime" through an evening and then "No reachable dry shore found", with dry floor a block away: the shore search only takes landings under open sky, and the pit is under the base's roof. With no landing even tried, the bot now climbs onto the nearest dry cell with a floor and air (forward and jumping, as out of lava). The flooding itself (the pond running into a pit beside the furnace) is still to be dealt with at the base.

89. *Four minutes in one cell looking for endermen.* Every walk of the enderman search from a hillside ledge ended "navigation timed out without reaching new ground", and every failure went to persist, which turns the search: a frontier search to its next heading, but a ring search (no heading) was dropped outright, so the next search began again here on its first leg, the walk that had just failed. A ring search is now turned to its next leg from the same origin.

90. *Every question in one place.* At the user's request (the peer audit's proposal), the fifty-seven questions Jev is asked moved into `src/decisions`: survival, work, combat, travel, intake, build, dream and command modules, each question defined once with its stakes, its bar and what happens below it, and for the in-game trees the code's own answer when Jev is unreachable. One runner, `decide`, asks every tree (the abort watcher, the outage walk and announcement, the staleness and validity checks and the decision log were copied at five call sites and had drifted: idle, stillness and house steps were charged to the ledger as "source"); `ask` composes every batch. New bars: the survival priority, the ranged response and the dragon fight fall back to their code order below 0.2, and the four command steps that had neither bar nor fallback (node, argument, subject, destination) ask the player below 0.5. Tests hold that no file outside `src/decisions` calls the model, that every high-stakes question has a bar or says why not, and that `docs/decisions.md`, generated from the definitions, is current. 875 tests; the drills pass bar the noisy blaze damage.

91. *Exploring, and remembering what is out there.* At the user's suggestion, exploring is an idle activity: the nearest unexplored 64-block area around home, one leg of up to a minute and a half, an area it cannot reach resting half an hour. Every trip counts, not only exploring: the main loops mark the area the bot stands in (with its biome) and look for landmarks on the village scan's cheap schedule. Landmarks, each with its own detector: ruined portals (crying obsidian, or obsidian beside netherrack or magma), surface lava pools (obsidian from a water bucket), dungeons (a spawner in cobblestone), mineshafts (rails near cobwebs), desert and jungle temples, and in the Nether fortresses (twenty-four bricks, not the bot's own few) and bastions (blackstone with gold, for bartering). Villages keep their own fuller record. All of it goes in the per-world store, so every later goal knows it; a first sighting is said in chat. First use: the fortress search walks to a remembered fortress instead of sweeping for one.

92. *Putting the landmarks to use, and sprinting.* The obsidian step, with no lava in view, now walks to a remembered ruined portal (its frame is obsidian to mine, with a diamond pickaxe) or a remembered surface lava pool before digging a shaft down to the lava lakes. The pearl rung, in the Nether with no gold to throw and a bastion remembered, goes for the bastion's gold: a gold block is nine ingots, and only a block no piglin is within sixteen of is mined, because piglins turn on a player who mines gold in their sight, gold armour or not. In chat, "what have you found?" and "where is the nearest village?" are answered from the world's findings, and "go to the village" walks there; they are never forgotten, being the world's and not the player's. Sprinting: until now only the escape and the End evasion sprinted. Long trips over open ground (exploring, a remembered landmark, the portal leg, going home for the night, a village, a saved place) now sprint while hunger is above fourteen, about a third faster at a hunger point every forty blocks; fights, ledges and tunnels still walk.

93. *Death eighteen: ran from an enderman.* The pearl hunt dug down to an enderman in a cave; one enderman, four damage a hit through iron. Jev swung twice, then ran (it teleports after a runner), held, and ate at five health: twenty to one in five seconds. With an enderman within eight blocks and nothing else near, it is now fought where it stands while health is eight or more (closing on it when out of reach), and below that sealed out in a two-high pocket a three-high enderman cannot stand in. New arena drill `enderman_single` (a hunt for the pearl): before, five runs, no deaths, median 4.1 damage, the worst 20.3 with an escape in it; after, six runs, no deaths, median 4.1, the worst 12.2 and no escapes.

94. *Death nineteen: ate twice with a zombie beside it.* Climbing out of a mineshaft at full health, Jev met a skeleton and two zombies at three to five blocks, dug a bunker while they hit it (17 to 9), and then ate twice at six and seven health with a zombie at arm's length; eating is a second and a half standing still, and what it restores comes back over the next minute. No eating now with a hostile within five blocks, unless starving. The first live "ruined portal" was also Jev's own portal frame beside its own netherrack; its own portals are now excluded. New arena drill `cave_trio` (a skeleton and two zombies in a corridor): no deaths before or after, 6.3 and 6.4 damage; the open corridor does not reproduce the dark mineshaft, so the eating rule rests on the recording.

95. *Phantoms (from a player's review of the decision page).* Phantoms spawn over a player who has not slept in three in-game days, and nothing counted nights awake: the run spends many nights sealed in or night mining, and "stay up" was on offer every night the bot was armed and had a bed. The world tick of the last sleep is now kept, and after two days awake staying up is off the table while a bed is on offer, so the bed is taken. With no bed about, a sealed pocket or a mine is still the night, and phantoms only come under open sky. The same review found the rest largely sound; the open points it raised that hold up are caged End crystals (no build-up option), food breadth (no fishing) and the shield in the ranged response.

96. *Nether gold ore in the Overworld.* The golden-boots rung stood in the Overworld planning to mine twenty-six nuggets' worth of nether gold ore. The planner's cost estimate treats anything carried as free, so ten nuggets made the ingot-from-nuggets recipe beat smelting raw gold, and the rest of the nuggets had only one source, in another dimension. The planner now knows the dimension it plans in: a source found only in another dimension costs a trip, and a handful (under thirty-two) of something whose only source is elsewhere is not free. In the Overworld the boots are now gold ore, smelted; in the Nether, nether gold ore; with forty nuggets carried, the nuggets.

97. *The chests the world leaves lying about.* The user saw a chest beside a ruined portal and nothing opened chests but the stash. Now a chest within twenty-four blocks that belongs to a remembered ruined portal, dungeon or temple is opened as a rule, and what the ladder uses (gold, obsidian, flint and steel, golden apples and armour, iron, diamonds, bread, the keepsakes and the kit) is taken; the rest is left and the chest recorded, per world, so it is not opened twice. A trip to a remembered structure with unopened chests is an idle option. Left alone: any chest at home or placed by the bot, a chest with TNT beside it (the desert temple trap), and everything in the Nether (a bastion's lid angers the piglins). The chest in question turned out to be Jev's own stash, nine blocks from its own portal, whose cobblestone corners make it look ruined: not loot, and the rule leaves it. The dungeon Jev remembers at (-365, 38, -167) is the first real candidate. Mineshaft chests ride in minecarts and are not yet opened.

98. *A batch of one beef, for good.* The saved furnace batch was one beef whose furnace had gone cold and empty; finishing it meant getting beef again, which the planner has no method for, and "I'll keep trying" ran to attempt seven, after an earlier stretch of "I can't reach the furnace holding our saved batch" that could never resolve either. A saved batch with nothing in its furnace and none of its input carried is let go, and so is one whose furnace is out of reach three times running.

99. *The portal room was one block past the look.* In the endgame rehearsal (`.test-endgame`, seed 2026092301) the eyes led to within seven blocks of the stronghold's start and the search then ran out its twenty-five minutes: the portal room lay forty-five blocks sideways and seventy down, ninety-seven blocks in a line, and the frame look reached ninety-six. Within ninety-six blocks of the eyes' estimate the look now reaches a hundred and sixty. The drill passed at the next run: four throws, the estimate on the true start, the portal at (85, -18, 2021) in 9.7 minutes, nineteen eyes left, no deaths.

100. *A portal ring over a cave.* The rehearsal's portal room had been cut through by a cave: the frames hung over open air, the silverfish stairs on the east the only floor, and the eyes for the three far frames had nowhere to be placed from ("no observed dry route" within six seconds, eight eyes in). When no standing room beside a frame can be walked to, footing is laid now: an open cell outside the ring and within reach, the pathfinder allowed its scaffolding for that approach only. `enter_end` passed at the next run, in the End in twelve seconds with seventeen eyes left.

101. *The dragon drill passed, and had not.* Its first run "won" in a minute with two arrows and every crystal standing: the drill counted the dragon dead when the client stopped seeing it, and it had flown out of tracking range. The pass is now the server's word, the `end/kill_dragon` advancement, asked every twenty seconds. The honest runs then showed three things. From the spawn platform the bot walked onto the island and stood at its edge with nothing to choose: every survey for a firing position had a quarter of a second and timed out on the nine-block climb to the pillars (now a second, and an empty choice records why it was empty). Knocked to the far side of a floorless portal ring, it could not get back to the one walkable edge (the edge is now reached by laying footing too). And with both fixed it reached the pillars, shot seventeen arrows and destroyed its first crystal, until an enderman, provoked by a glance, fought it to 2.6 health on the island and it died of the fall.

102. *Endermen, and a dodge that ended the fight.* The pathfinder walks with a level gaze, and a level gaze toward an enderman twenty blocks off is within three degrees of its eyes: near an unprovoked enderman the bot now walks looking at the ground ahead (src/gaze.js), whatever is pressing the keys, the dragon dodge included. The drill now logs the moment an enderman turns, and that showed the gaze is only part of it: four turned at ninety to a hundred and nineteen blocks, and two clusters came with the bow, one while aiming up at crystals and one straight after a crystal's explosion, whose damage the endermen blame on the archer. With the gaze lowered the next run had no death, ten arrows and two crystals down in 2.4 minutes, and ended at full health because one dragon charge had no walkable escape and the dodge called that Blocked. It is a transient failure now: the next step looks again.

103. *Dusk in a hollow, five hundred blocks out.* Exploring had taken Jev to (-687, 47, 269), eleven blocks under the surface, with no bed (lost with a death) and night coming. The shelter step surveyed every candidate site for a hundred and fifty milliseconds each, every survey timed out, and "No reachable, supported 3 by 3 shelter site" ran to attempt seven. Six sites at six hundred milliseconds now, and with none reachable the pocket is sealed where the bot stands, as the unreachable-shelter rule already meant.

104. *The rehearsal measured a fight the run never has.* The game loop hands the End to the dragon fight and skips the survival layer there; the drill ran the survival layer first on every tick, so every enderman fight and dig-in it recorded was one the run would not make, and in the run an enderman that turned would have met nothing at all. One such dig-in held the bot still in the dragon's breath at 7.6 health. The drill now mirrors the loop, and the End fight answers a turned mob within six blocks with the sword itself, under its own emergency checks, so breath or a fireball still hands over to the dodge; with no safe place because of mobs alone it holds with the sword out for a second instead of stopping. Across the runs the dragon has gone from 200 to 121.5. The provocation log also shows endermen turning at sixty to seventy-eight blocks while the bot looked at the ground: not the gaze, likely grudges kept from earlier drill runs in the same End, so some enderman fights in the End are unavoidable and have to be won.

105. *Water against endermen, and a bucket for each job.* An enderman that touches water is hurt and teleports away, so a turned one within twelve blocks now meets a bucket poured into the bot's own cell, whose flowing ring it has to cross to strike; the water is taken back once none is near. The first run with it went 5.8 minutes, no death, the dragon from 121.5 to 102.25, and ended because the one bucket was out on the ground when the dragon's knockback lifted the bot, and fall recovery called a waterless fall Blocked. The End supplies are two buckets now, water is poured only with two carried, and a fall with none is simply waited out.

106. *The run moves to a recorded server (2026-09-23 05:54 UTC).* At the user's request the dream run continues on `.test-replay/` (port 25580), a Fabric server with ServerReplay that records Jev from join to leave for rendering in ReplayMod. Its world is a copy of `natural-20260921-b` taken about three minutes before the move, so those minutes of play on 25579 are lost; the bot's own state was copied across, and Jev rejoined at (-630, 36, 254) carrying on where it left off. Nothing else about the run changed: same code, same kit-restore aid (now pointed at the new console), same supervisor.

107. *Free the End (2026-09-23 06:04 UTC, rehearsal).* The last death before it: seven endermen screamed within 0.6 seconds as the dragon charged through them, the bot counted all seven its own, fought them and looked at their heads while it waited, which is what turns one on a player. A screaming enderman is angry at something, not necessarily the bot: the dragon's charge turns them on the dragon. It is now the bot's enemy only if it has hurt the bot in the last twenty seconds or come within four blocks; the End fight looks at an enderman's legs while it waits for one, and the gaze stays down near every enderman, screaming or not. The next run killed the dragon: 6.9 minutes, fifty arrows, no death, the server's `end/kill_dragon` advancement at 02:04:34 local. The dragon had been worn down across the earlier runs (200 to 68.75, with the crystals shot out in the first ones), so this is the fight finished, not yet a fight won from the platform in one go; that is the next rehearsal, on a fresh End.

108. *Turned fourteen hundred times in a pocket, and a goal with no isValid.* On the replay server the bot sealed a pocket among the lava pools at (-577, 55, 258), and the night mine found water or lava behind the rock on every heading: each branch was refused at once and the mine turned to the next, heading 1488 by the time it was seen. Four headings refused within a minute with nothing dug now box the mine in for ten minutes, and the night is waited out in the pocket. Before that, the process crashed three times in the physics tick with "stateGoal.isValid is not a function": something handed the pathfinder a goal without the methods it calls every tick. The caller was lost with the log each restart overwrites, so every goal set on the pathfinder is now made whole first (session.js wraps setGoal) and the caller is logged as a [bug] with its stack.

109. *The crash was a name.* With the goal guard injected after the pathfinder at last, the fifth occurrence was caught and named: `drop-collection.js` walks straight at a drop the router could not claim with the motion helper `move`, but `collectNearbyDrops` takes a `move` option (the pathfinder's navigate) that shadowed it, so a key-press spec went to the pathfinder as its goal. The helper is now called by its module. The guard also refuses anything that is not a destination at all, so a walk like that fails as an ordinary error instead of the next physics tick taking the process down.

110. *Free the End, on tape (2026-09-23 07:09 UTC).* The rehearsal server now runs Fabric with ServerReplay and records EndgameJev. On a fresh End with the frames emptied, the recorded dragon fell across three files: `02-32-32.mcpr` (the stronghold in 2.5 minutes, a teleport to the platform after the portal step stopped a block short of a frame's reach, since fixed, then twenty-five minutes of fight: every crystal down, the dragon to 54.75, no death), `03-01-26.mcpr` (to 26, then an enderman traded blows with a sword that never raised its shield, 20 health to none in six seconds; the End defence now swings on the sword's cooldown with the shield up between, as the survival layer does), and `03-05-35.mcpr` (the finish: twenty arrows, 3.9 minutes, `end/kill_dragon` at 03:09:44 local, no death). An earlier attempt, `02-10-36.mcpr`, died at 115 walking under an enderman's blows; walks now stop for a mob at arm's length. Not yet one unbroken run from the eyes to the dragon: the portal walk-in is the piece still to show.

111. *An hour on the first rung with two diamond pickaxes.* The ladder counts a tool spent below a fifth of its durability; a fifth of a diamond pickaxe is three hundred uses, so at two hundred and two each Jev's pair counted as nothing and the stone-pickaxe rung came round again. The stone one it made went straight back on the ground, because the full-pockets tidy dropped the spare tool before the forty-six nether brick fences on the junk list. A tool is sound now at a fifth or sixty-four uses, whichever is less, and room is made from junk before tools.

112. *Death on the stairs, 2026-09-23 07:29 UTC (replay server).* A zombie followed the gold staircase down and hit for 1.38 through iron once a second for fourteen seconds; the bot "fought" the whole time and never landed a swing. The flight recorder showed its height bouncing 38, 39, 40: every swing began with the jump for a critical, the jump landed a step up the stair, the zombie below was out of reach and the swing came back "missed". A crit jump that comes down out of reach is not tried again at that mob for eight seconds; the swings are plain. Kit restored by the aid, labelled.

113. *Two hoglins, and a pillar the server refused.* The replay run died to a pair of hoglins on open Nether ground, 20 health to none in nine seconds. A new arena drill, `hoglin_pair` (two on open ground), reproduced it: two deaths in three, digging in while both hit. Two hoglins or more within ten blocks are now met from two blocks up (hoglins cannot climb, and a player two up is out of their reach). The first try at it never left the ground: every block was refused, "the block is still air", because mineflayer's placeBlock turns smoothly to look before it places and the bot fell back into the cell during the turn. Pillar blocks are now placed with the look already down (forceLook). After: `hoglin_pair` three of three, no deaths, no damage; `hoglin_herd` the same; `hoglin_single` cleared three of three with 12.4 damage, inside the drill's noise. The pillar fix reaches every pillar the bot builds, the dream run's portal and tunnel climbs included.

114. *A climb home that failed once a second.* Sixteen blocks under the bed at dusk, the go-home rule called the surface climb every tick; the climb threw, the error was swallowed, and the step reported that it had acted, so for minutes the bot bobbed between y 47 and 48 with nothing in the log but stillness. Three failed climbs in a minute now set the walk home aside for ten minutes, and the night is spent the other ways. The same stretch of the run had four Nether deaths in seventy minutes (two to hoglins, one to zombified piglins after the pillar held, one to a skeleton that shot for forty-eight seconds with no survival response at all); those are the next pass, not fixed here.

115. *Walled into a flooded cave; a second controlled aid.* Sixteen blocks under the base the bot swam for about an hour in a flooded cave at (-434, 47, -150): a pool from y 44 to 48 under a stone roof, dry air three blocks east behind a stone wall. The shore search only takes routes that neither dig nor place, so it offered a swim to a landing it never reached. A dig-to-shore fallback was added (nearest dry cell within ten blocks, water allowed to flow, never lava), and its record showed every dig route coming back "noPath" with dry cells a block away, cause not yet found. The cave's blocks were captured (scripts/lib/flooded-cave.json) for a terrain drill, and at the user's word Jev was teleported by console to the surface at the base (-428, 63, -156). This is a controlled-run aid of a new kind, beside the kit restore, and labelled as such.

116. *The flooded cave, rebuilt and dug out of.* The cave was captured from the live world and rebuilt as a terrain drill (`flooded_cave`: each water cell laid as a source, every solid block stone; water breathing given, since the rebuild lacks the live pool's air pockets and the question is the way out). The drill reproduced the hour exactly and three causes came out one by one. The vitals rule swam up for air whenever the head was under water, full air bar or not, and under a roof that failed every tick and starved everything else: after a failed swim, only low air sends it up again for a minute. The shore search's float to the surface threw under the roof and ended the search, and water filled to the roof threw "no open surface" 783 times in two minutes: both now go to the dig. And the dig itself came back "noPath" to dry cells a block away because it ran under the surface rules, which add a cost of a hundred to breaking anything but leaves; a probe in the rebuilt cave showed the same routes succeeding with the rules lifted. The dig now lifts them. `flooded_cave` passes two of two, out and dry in about 105 seconds (mining under water is slow).

117. *The Nether deaths, a first pass.* Three of the four. Zombified piglins: a sword's full swing on the ground sweeps every mob within a block of the target, and a sweep that touches a neutral zombified piglin turns the group; beside one, the swing is now made with a pickaxe or an axe (only swords sweep), or as a critical, or held. The new arena drill `hoglin_bystanders` (a hoglin with two zombified piglins beside it) went from 16.4 damage to 9.6, no deaths. Hoglins: the pillar went up with a hoglin already at arm's length, which threw the bot off it; it now goes up at two in sight within sixteen blocks, or one within ten once health is below fourteen. `hoglin_pair` 0 damage, `hoglin_single` 8.9 and cleared, `hoglin_bystanders` 8.8, all three of three without a death. The skeleton that shot for forty-eight seconds with no response left nothing to find it by; now three hurts in fifteen seconds with no survival action log a [bug] line with the step and every threat in view (distance, visibility, hunted, the fight's target), so the next one says why.

118. *A restock that could not finish, again and again.* At the base the run cycled through restocks the chest could not complete (a furnace forty times over, flagged each time by the no-progress watchdog, then planned again at once). A restock that makes no measurable progress now sets its items aside for fifteen minutes, and the restock plan leaves them out.

119. *What the landmarks are for.* A mineshaft was remembered and then ignored. Now its chests are looted where they ride, in minecarts (entities, opened like a chest), passed over when a spawner is within ten blocks (cave spiders), and with no cobweb in view a remembered mineshaft is where the search for string goes. A Nether fortress's chests are opened too, never a bastion's and never with a piglin in sight; and a village's. The new terrain drill `mineshaft_cart` (a rail tunnel with a chest minecart) passes, and caught a record bug on the way: mineflayer updates an open window's copy of the pockets, not the bot's, so every loot had been recorded as taking nothing; the count now reads the window's own slots.

120. *Enchanting.* New (src/enchanting.js): the sword, then the bow, then the armour from the chest down, then the pickaxe, iron or better, each at the enchanting table with the dearest offer the levels and lapis pay for (no bookshelves yet, so I and II). It is an idle option whenever a table is known, lapis is carried and the level is five or more, and before the End the supplies step builds the table and enchants what the levels allow. The planner could not make the table: its only leather was rabbit hide; cows are now a leather source. The drill `enchant_table` found three mineflayer details in a row: the pockets are numbered differently inside the table's window; closing the window copies a stale view of the pockets unless it is synced first (the sword and lapis vanished to the client); and in 26.1 an item's enchantments come as { enchantments: [{ id, level }] }, not a list. It passes two of two now: Sharpness or Knockback on the sword, a Protection on the chestplate, ten levels down to four.

121. *Trading, and experience for the table.* New (src/trading.js): at a remembered village the adult villagers in reach are opened and their offers read (kept ten minutes); spare coal, sticks, wheat, rotten flesh, paper, iron, leather and string are sold for emeralds, and what the run needs is bought: ender pearls from a cleric first, arrows, a bow, armour or tools better than what is carried, food when the reserve is low. Jev picks the trade (registered as trade_choice); without Jev, a wanted buy first. It is an idle option whenever a village is remembered and there is something to trade, and the pearl rung trades instead of hunting endermen once a cleric's pearls have been read. The drill `village_trade` (a fletcher, a cleric and a coal buyer with fixed offers) first spent its three emeralds on arrows in front of a cleric asking five for a pearl; a pearl on offer that cannot yet be paid for now keeps the emeralds for it and the sales go on. After: sticks, coal, pearl, two of two. And experience for enchanting: an idle option smelts the raw ore carried (the stash holds a hundred raw iron), while there is gear to enchant and the level is under thirty.

122. *A staircase set aside, chosen again.* The mining step persisted nineteen times at "the staircase toward (-625, 28, 308) is set aside; trying another way": the tunnel rested the target, but the mining step picked the nearest ore in view without looking at the set-aside list, the same ore each time. The ore it was for (and anything of its kind within four blocks) now rests twenty minutes when its staircase does, and the mining step's choice skips what is resting.

123. *A working night mine taken for a restless one.* The user asked why Jev sheltered instead of mining. The pocket dig-out rule (three dig-outs in two minutes set the night mine aside) counted every successful mining step since my earlier fix, and a mine digging down or inward never opens the pocket: three "dig-outs" 1.4 seconds apart after three blocks, and the night was waited out. A dig-out is now counted only when the step leaves the pocket unsealed. Phantoms are the sleep-debt rule's business (the bed on the third night), and mining under rock is out of their way.

124. *A day, audited, and what a day costs.* scripts/audit-day.js reads the flight recorder for a window and reports every stretch that looks wrong (standing still, in water, pacing, steps flipping, retry loops, errors, damage, items thrown away), where the time went, and the day's Jev inference. The cost, from the ledger's per-game-day tally over seventy-nine days of the dream run: about 22 Jev calls and 28,000 tokens a game day on average (a quiet day 1 to 10 calls; the worst, day 34, 367 calls and 341,000 tokens, survival questions asked over and over); at Jev's published $0.042 per million input tokens (output free) that is about a tenth of a cent a game day, a cent and a half on the worst, and about nine cents for the whole run so far. The largest single item is recovery advice at about 13,000 tokens a call, fifteen times an ordinary decision.

125. *The first audited day (2026-09-23 14:06 to 14:26 UTC).* No deaths, no damage, no time in water; the night mined (the fix of note 123 held); one Jev call all day. The audit flagged: about six minutes on four gold ingots (a smelt of four with one raw gold, a staircase for more, the saved batch's furnace unreachable on the way back until the rule let it go); pacing near the mine; a minute and a half standing at a furnace while it worked; and sixteen "timed out waiting for world/inventory update" with "invalid operation" retries, in crafting and smelting. The last reproduced in a drill: `craft_cycle` on vanilla made eight stone pickaxes in nine seconds without an error, and `craft_full_pockets`, the same with the pockets full of junk the way the run carries them, made one in a hundred and fifty seconds with thirty-three timeouts: the crafted item had nowhere to go. Crafting and smelting now make room for their output first (junk before tools, the recipe's ingredients kept); after, four in five seconds, no errors.

126. *A bed in sight, no plan to use it.* The second night after the fix of note 123 was still eleven minutes of waiting, reason "waiting for daylight", no watcher. The night mine refuses while a bed is carried or in sight during sleeping hours (the night belongs to the bed), but that night's plan was a shelter: the bed in view was out of reach and none was carried, so the bot neither slept nor mined. The bed now defers the mine only when the night's plan is to sleep.

127. *The first audit's open items.* The six minutes on four gold ingots: a new smelt batch is now only as large as the raw input carried, so nothing is left waiting in a furnace while the bot digs for the rest, and the rest is smelted as a batch of its own at whatever furnace is nearest. Standing beside a working furnace: while a batch has more than fifteen seconds to run, an ore within arm's reach of where the bot stands is dug meanwhile, the furnace shut and opened again (the drill `furnace_wait`: eight ingots, three ores dug while waiting, eighty-one seconds). The pacing near the mine was the mine step walking twelve blocks up its own staircase toward a block the record did not name, and the tunnel's return to its worksite bringing it back down, twice; the mine step now records its target and where it set out from, for the next audit to say which block.

128. *The second audit's full pockets.* Thirty-four kinds in thirty-six slots, and three causes under "have 0 of 4, 0 free slots". The room made for an output was picked straight back up: cobblestone went 189, 64, 125 at a crafting table and 128, 64, 128 at a furnace, dropped at the bot's feet where the work stood. Drops are now thrown upward and away from the workstation (or behind the way the bot faces). The slot made before the furnace opened was filled by the dirt from the furnace's own footing while the batch cooked; room is now checked again at the moment the output is taken, the window closed to make it and opened again, and an ore is dug while waiting only with two slots to spare. And little counted as junk: nether wart (the run does not brew), an egg, spider eyes, and netherrack outside the Nether now go before cobblestone; seeds, copper and rotten flesh go last. A fourth finding under the same timeouts: the golden boots were crafted and then tossed twice as spare boots, no better than the iron ones worn, which is why the golden-boots rung ran out its time. One pair of golden boots is never a spare now.

129. *Strategy is Jev's now.* The ladder's order was code, and a day could go to one rung: twenty minutes digging for gold with a ruined portal's chest remembered nearby. A new decision, `win_strategy` (src/strategy.js), offers Jev the rungs open now (the ladder's next and each later one that may wait; pickaxes and armour never skip) and, by day and in good health, the side trips: loot a remembered structure, trade at a remembered village, enchant at a known table. The ladder's next rung is the fallback. The choice holds until the rung or the options change or ten minutes pass; a side trip runs once and rests ten minutes. A live probe of four cases: loot when the boots had stalled eighteen minutes with a portal chest ninety blocks off, enchant with fourteen levels and a plain diamond sword, the boots when just begun (once the diamond sword instead, a fair call). The miss was a 240-block walk to a chest with half a minute of daylight left, and that is arithmetic: a trip is now offered only if there and back fits in the daylight, and Jev is told the daylight in minutes.

130. *The third audit (15:37-15:57, the pockets fix live).* No inventory timeouts, the golden boots made and kept, and the ladder moved on: valuables stashed, the Nether entered, the fortress sweep begun. Two deaths. In a cave, three creepers, a skeleton and a zombie at four to seven blocks, and the cornered rules began an eighteen-cell pocket; a blast took seventeen health before it closed. A creeper within seven blocks now sends the bot away from the creepers first (footing that gains room from them alone), and no pocket or bunker is begun or offered while one is that close. In the Nether, a hoglin's knockback on a ledge dropped the bot fifteen blocks into the lava sea, where no dry cell lay within three blocks and the lava step did nothing, returning every eight milliseconds for four seconds while the bot burned. The exit search now reaches six blocks and five up, and with none in sight the bot swims up and back toward its last dry footing. Hoglin knockback beside Nether drops (both falls that day) is still open. And seven "I'm having trouble thinking" lines were a stash error that listed lapis lazuli: "lapis" matched /API/i. The outage words are whole words now. Jev inference for the day: three calls, 2,523 tokens.

131. *The full stash and the hoglin's toss.* The nine stash_valuables retries before the Nether were a full chest: 216 coal in four stacks, 103 raw iron, 99 lapis and nineteen other kinds in twenty-seven slots, and every deposit failed silently while the ladder asked again.
