Asked at a real early choice (trial 92, iron pickaxe made, shield next), Jev rated it 0.01 and went on to the shield and the iron: with iron in reach, copper is a detour. It stays an option, not a rule.
79. *Animals remembered (the user: "remember the animals").* Cows, pigs and chickens are noted with the sheep, a herd to a place, for half an hour. The food choices offer the nearest three herds out of view ("walk back to where 3 cow were seen 2 minutes ago, 90 blocks east"), a hunt says how many of its kind are known about ("killing it leaves one cow known nearby; a pen needs two to breed"), and the home offers fetching a remembered herd to the pen when none is in view. A herd not reached, or reached and gone, is forgotten.
80. *Trial 94, swimming for the sheep (the user: "really failing to swim to the sheep").* On an island, Jev swam west for sheep on the next one, and each time the walk paused in the water the work loop's rule, "work starts on dry ground", took the nearest shore: the one it had left. Three times in ninety seconds. Every walk now records where it is heading; a swim headed somewhere more than sixteen blocks off in the last twenty seconds, head above water and air full, is left alone by that rule, and the walk goes on. (Weighing the landings by the heading instead did not work: the block search returns its first 256 in chunk order, and the far shore was not among them.)
81. *Trial 92 passed; trial 94 held its walk home all night doing nothing.* Trial 92 had all six by minute 22 (bed 4, pickaxe and shield 16, sword 17, armour 19, home 22) and went the hour without a death or a loop. Trial 94 chose the walk home thirty blocks from its bed after bedtime. The walk was written for dusk: past bedtime it did nothing, and the held plan came back to it twenty times a second, so the sleep option was never offered. A chosen walk home now walks at any hour. Then at dawn the armour was offered "all of it from what is carried: no gathering" with eleven raw iron in the pockets. Each piece had been planned alone against the same pockets, and each fit. A set is now planned as one batch: mine 13 iron ore, smelt 24.
82. *Trial 99, a bare-handed fight with a creeper that struck nothing.* With no blade (its logs had gone on a stone pickaxe, then a food hunt and a night mined underground), at ten health, Jev chose to fight a creeper at arm's length. The fight's swing gave any creeper within five blocks to the creeper dance and returned, and the dance runs only with a blade: the fight came back empty twenty times a second. The swing now gives a close creeper to the dance, and where the dance cannot run, swings. Trials 95 to 97 are void: TypeSafe credits ran out at 14:39 UTC and every decision failed until the user added more.
83. *Trial 101 burned; trial 98's chest blown up.* A minute and three quarters in, trial 101 went for an oak beside the lava pool it had found, the forest caught, and it stood in the burning grass from twenty health to nothing in twenty seconds with nothing answering the hurt. In fire, or burning beside it, the bot now runs on the keys to the nearest cell two blocks clear of any fire, or into water, through one cell of flame when ringed (the path search will not start from fire or cross it, and returned at once). Replayed on the arena, ringed by fire on netherrack: burned 1/1 standing still, alive 3/3 after, at sixteen health or more. Chat says "Ahh, I'm on fire! Getting out of it." The user watched a creeper blow up trial 98's chest by the bed, on a dark mountainside; it was empty. Torches around home were offered only once the plot and the pen were done too: they are now offered from the bed and the chest on, with a lost chest said.
84. *Trial 98, a chest over a crater.* After the creeper, the chest's cell had the blast's hole under it and the pond's water running in beside it: "no adjacent solid anchor" three times, and the trial failed on the loop with five of six by minute fifteen. Open cells under the chest (up to three) are now laid with a building block from the bottom up before the chest goes down. Trial 100 had all six by minute 36.
85. *A wall round home, Jev's to choose (the user: "add wall as an option").* From the bed and the chest on, beside the torches: a ring two blocks high one block outside the levelled footprint, round the bed, the chest, the plot, the pen and the water, each column built up from its own ground (a dip filled, a rock left as it is, a cliff more than four down left as its own wall), with a door by the bed on ground at the home's level. Said with its cost (blocks carried, blocks to dig, a door to make, minutes) and what it keeps out (zombies cannot break a door on Normal; a creeper has to blow in from outside; spiders still climb). Trial 100 passed: all six by minute 36, no death, no loop.
86. *Trial 102, a block of its own on the pen's fence line.* All six by minute 28, then the pen: a cobblestone of the bot's own (a night shell's) on a fence cell, "placement obstructed by cobblestone" five times, and the trial failed on the loop at minute 35 (the user saw it struggling). The pen cleared its own blocks off the gate and the cell before it, not off the fence line; it now clears them off every fence cell it is about to fill. The Creative bot on 25570 had tried a build blocked by an oak slab it had not placed 5,630 times in a day and a half: a build blocked by a block someone else put there now waits on the player, says so once, and goes on by itself when the block is gone.
87. *Trial 104, slain by a mob on no list.* Twenty-one minutes in, mining at y -10 with a bed, the iron pickaxe, the shield and the iron sword, the bot was hit five times by a zombie villager with "no survival response": the threat list, the hostile names written out by hand, had no zombie villager on it, and the hits came from nothing it could see. Every mob the game calls hostile is now a threat (zombie villagers, vexes, guardians, the creaking, illusioners, and the parched, a skeleton that shoots), the neutral ones still left to what provokes them.
88. *Trial 106, the fight set aside at arm's length.* Seven minutes in, digging a staircase down at fifteen health with a stone sword, a zombie followed it down. Jev chose to fight (0.96); the charge could not climb the stairs to it at three and a half blocks and the fight counted as a stance that failed, set aside for twenty seconds against zombies. The zombie came down to one block, and Jev was offered a pillar, a retreat and a pocket in a one-wide staircase: dead from fifteen health in nine seconds. A fight set aside for want of reach is offered again once a mob is at reach.
89. *Trials 105 to 111.* 105 (swamp), 107 (snowy taiga) and 109 missed the bed or the home by minute 45: no sheep near, flocks gone when walked back to, a desert chosen to look for sheep in; 108 had five by minute 21 and chose exploring over levelling the home. Home or bed missing by minute 45 is now the commonest failure (94, 103, 105, 107, 108, 109); whether Jev is told the minute-45 target is the user's call. 111 had all six by minute 22 and died at minute 49: mining through the night at y 0, three minutes at five to seven health with hunger at sixteen and seventeen (no healing below eighteen) and the last food eaten, it chose the walk to a remembered herd in the dark at seven health, met a skeleton and two zombies, charged the skeleton, and was shot. Judgments, each with its facts; no single rule broke.
90. *A bed without sheep (the user: what do players do?).* Igloos are noticed (a bed among snow blocks) and remembered with their bed, and the bed step takes one the way it takes a village bed. The sheep search offers cutting the cobwebs in view with the sword when string is still wanted (one string a web, four a wool), and tells Jev the ways without sheep: spiders' string at night, cobwebs in mineshafts, igloos and village houses, and that phantoms only come after three nights without sleep.
91. *Trial 114, a crit jump beside a creeper.* Twenty-one minutes in, climbing out of a mine with no armour, a zombie and a creeper met it together: retreat, creeper dance, pillar, creeper dance; the swing reflex jumped for a critical hit on the zombie with the creeper under four blocks off, and the blast came mid-jump, from twelve health. No crit jump now with a creeper within five blocks; the dance is offered with what a blast costs (up to twenty-two without armour, about half in iron) beside the health and armour worn. Trial 112 missed the bed and the home by minute 45 (no sheep).
92. *Trial 113, the bed passed over for forty minutes.* In snow, the bed was the ladder's next step from the start and was offered as "searching for sheep for 43 minutes, none seen yet"; Jev chose the armour pieces, the iron pickaxe again, golden boots and a diamond sword instead, every time (0.95 against 0.04), and missed the bed and the home at minute 45. With no flock known, the bed is now offered with the other ways as they stand: the string carried of the twelve a bed takes, spiders at night and cobwebs, and an igloo with a bed when one is known.
93. *Never a chicken or a pig (the user: "Add one rule that's hard enforced - never hurt a chicken", "Or a pig").* Not Jev's to weigh: the one place a swing leaves the bot (bot.attack) refuses a chicken or a pig, and refuses a grounded sword swing that would sweep one standing within reach of the target (the swing picks an axe or a pickaxe first, as it does beside a piglin, or waits for a falling hit, which does not sweep); a bow is not drawn with one near the line of the shot. They are off every food, hunt and pickup list and are no longer noted as herds. Arrows come off skeletons now (their loot table added to the vanilla data): feathers have no other source.
94. *The midgame trial, and a dream run parked for sixteen hours.* scripts/midgame.js copies a world a first-days trial passed on and resumes that trial's own bot from its saved state; it passes on the Nether, a fortress, six blaze rods and twelve pearls with no death and no loop (at most three hours). mid-100-a died in five minutes: trial 100's bot had ended its hour with three rotten flesh and no other food, the copy woke at night, it could not heal, and zombies in its mine took it. The dream run on 25580 had been "blocked" since 02:10 UTC: a "Threat nearby" that escaped the goal's loop reached the session's last catch, which parked the goal for good. A threat or a breath that escapes now sends the goal round again a second and a half later (five in a minute still parks it).
95. *Trial 115, seventeen blocks under home.* In its own mine under the base, the walk home came back "no path" twenty-five times and flipped with a detour until the audit called the loop. With no path, the way home is now dug a stretch at a time (the tunnel step every way back to a portal uses: a pillar when home is overhead and near, else a staircase), and walked again, twelve rounds at most. The survival layer's chores get the same way.
96. *mid-110-a, obsidian by a staircase.* Nineteen minutes in, the portal's obsidian took it down a staircase toward a lava lake at y -54; each round of making obsidian dug a stretch of tunnel, and the step was named "make_obsidian" and "tunnel" in turn every few seconds while the shaft went down a block every four or five. The audit called it a flip (each fifteen-second window moved just under three blocks). A staircase dug inside another step now keeps that step's name, the tunnel its phase.
97. *Trial 118, two creepers told as nothing.* Eleven minutes in, no armour, twelve health, two creepers and a spider: Jev chose to fight, offered "about 7.5 seconds and 6.7 damage", numbers that leave creepers out (a blast is once, not a rate); one went off, the second followed the backing bot at two blocks and went off too. The arena's creeper drills pass in iron (pair: no death, 5 damage median; follow: 0.6), so the dance is sound; what was missing was the fact. The fight is now offered with each creeper's blast beside its numbers (up to 22 after the armour worn, and "more than the bot has" when it is). Trial 115's regression server now runs a midgame world too.
98. *mid-110-b, burning with the water in its pack.* Getting obsidian at a lava pool, it stepped in and out and kept burning (lava leaves a body burning for a quarter of a minute), from thirteen health to two standing still, and later died by the same pool; a water bucket was in its pack for the obsidian. Burning with no fire about and a water bucket carried, the bot now pours it at its feet and takes it back (not in the Nether). Replayed on the arena, set burning and moved clear: lost 9 health doing nothing, 0 three times out of three with the bucket, the bucket kept.
99. *mid-110-c, a night hunt into a crowd.* Twelve minutes in, in iron with a shield, Jev took the bow rung at night (spiders for string) and chose it again with nine hostiles in view in a cave at y 32: a witch, three skeletons, spiders, a zombie and three creepers. Charge, retreat, pillar, fight, retreat: each stance a second or five, health from 19 to 1 in twenty seconds, a golden apple, and the witch's poison and harming finished it. No single rule broke; the choices were each Jev's with the counts in view.
100. *Six trials at once, and one shared log.* The dream server stopped; three more trial servers (25584 to 25586) run midgame worlds from trials 83, 87 and 100, with three watching clients over Jev's shoulder and scripts/watch-grid.js for all six. The three were started together, each read the one trial log, added itself and wrote it back, and the last write left only its own entry: the others would never have been judged. A trial is now one file (artifacts/midgame/<world>.json). mid-100-b (open): trial 100's bot woke underground without a pickaxe, wood or food, beside water; the staircase out backed off the water a hundred times, found no dry landing, and gave the turn to a one-move unstick that gave it back.
101. *mid-110-d and mid-100-b, a staircase that only backed off.* A flooded stair had no safe step; the tunnel backed off to a dry cell, found no step there either, and backed off again: the audit called the flip on one and the loop on the other. The count of "no way" reset whenever a retreat walked anywhere, so two cells could trade for ever. Four retreats with no step between now set the staircase aside, as three rounds with no progress do, and the caller tries another way.
102. *mid-92-a, the dirt that came back.* Seventy-three minutes in, in a one-wide tunnel at diamond depth with thirty-six slots full (four kinds of flower among them), it made room for a diamond by throwing its dirt along the way it faced: into the wall, onto its feet, and back into its pockets two seconds later (dirt 20, 4, then 15 within twenty seconds). "No room in my pockets for diamond" three times, and the loop. With no way to throw given, a stack now goes along the most open way at head height.
103. *Where the midgame's time goes.* Across six midgame trials, 84 of 220 bot-minutes were climbs to the surface (mid-87-a 26 of 42, mid-79-a 23 of 76): pickaxes worn through deep in the mine with no wood for another, then up for logs, some of it by hand. The wood reserve is six logs now (three pickaxes and a table, from three), and Jev is offered it with what running out costs where it stands: "about 43 blocks under the surface: a climb of 43 blocks, and back down".
104. *mid-110-e, the creeper behind the wall.* Thirty-four minutes in, in full iron, sealed in a pocket at night, it chose to open the pocket and go to its bed with two creepers nine and ten blocks off, and one blast took it from twenty. From inside a sealed pocket nothing is in sight, so the "watcher" named in the choice was nobody, and leaving and the bed were offered as if the way were clear. The mobs about that the wall hides are now named in the choice ("a creeper 10 blocks off (heard, not seen: the wall is between)").
105. *Flips caught as they happen (the user's pick of the general fixes).* Half the day's failures were two pieces of code handing the turn back and forth (staircase and retreat, obsidian and tunnel, the walk home and a detour, leaving a shelter and digging in), each fixed one pair at a time after the audit had failed the trial. The stall supervisor now runs the audit's own flip test every second, a little sooner (five changes between the same two actions in forty-five seconds, within five blocks, ending within three of the start, nothing worth keeping gained), over the work step and the survival layer's action alike, and raises it as a stall of the action in hand, which Jev answers as any stall: another way, a detour, or the step set aside. Two fight moves trading places are one fight. mid-92-b drowned digging for shore under a roof with its air running out; being looked into.
106. *The decision audit, first batch.* A read-only pass over every question Jev is asked, against a list of risk facts, found forty-eight gaps; the deadliest now said: every stance names the mobs about that are out of sight (it was built on the visible ones only); the fight names a creeper when it is the one it would close on; keep-working says a creeper at three blocks is already lighting; the pillar says who still reaches two up (spiders climb, endermen teleport, witches throw, a blast reaches); sealing says the mobs do not lose interest at night and how long to dawn; the fight estimate has every hostile (a ghast fight read "0 damage"), witches' harming through armour; the pocket's other doors (hunt, stash, cache, mine) name what is outside, the bed walk its seconds and the monsters by the bed that would refuse sleep, and "healing" only when hunger allows it; a night hunt names the other hostiles about, how far up or down, whether health comes back, and a creeper's blast; the work questions use the danger list (the Nether's mobs were missing); food walks give their seconds, the dark and whether health comes back. And mid-83-a, full of coal, dripstone and petals, chose "drop nothing" five times: making room lists what has no use and what is over its keeping first, and says what going without costs the step in hand. Flips are now looked for at every step change as well as every second (mid-110-f traded seven times in eight seconds, between looks).
107. *mid-92-c, a breaker that fired and changed nothing.* At the surface, a spider coming: dig in, and walled in the bot is "underground", so return to the surface, and dig in again, once a second for seven minutes, the work beneath trading mine and a stall detour. The live flip check raised it forty-four times; each raise set the survival action aside on the goal, and the survival layer only ever looked for its set-asides in its own state. A survival action set aside on the goal is refused now too (a hold such as digging in never is), so the layer falls through to its next answer.
108. *mid-92-b, drowned under a lily pad.* Floating in a pond at y 61.2 with its eyes seven hundredths under the surface: a lily pad overhead held its head down. The way to air went up through the pad, a block that breaks at a touch and so cost nothing and was named as nothing to dig; the bot pressed jump into its underside for thirteen seconds. A block with a collision box on the way to air is now dug however quickly it comes away, and a swimmer that gains no ground and no air for a second closes the cell it was making for and finds the way again from where it is. Replayed on the arena with the pond copied from the region file: drowned 2/2 before, breathing 3/3 after within three seconds.
109. *mid-79-a, a diamond with no room, four times.* A hundred and twenty minutes in, with gravel, dripstone, eggs, fence posts and three stacks of lapis in its pockets, "no room in my pockets for diamond" came back half a second at a time and nothing was thrown: making room returned "no room" whenever Jev's three rounds ended without room, whether Jev had said "drop nothing" or not. Only an explicit "nothing" goes without now; otherwise the tidy's own order makes the room. Jev's pick is logged.

## dream · 2026-09-21 · "your dream is to beat the game" on the Survival server · in progress

**So far.** Given the dream in chat, the win objective started with its expedition prep: a food reserve first. The bot walked 200 blocks hunting, got mutton, porkchop and chicken, made a wooden pickaxe and a crafting table, cooked once, and then paid a cobblestone trip for every further cook because the furnace stayed where it was placed. The gravel lip that ended trial two was passed on the way with a seven-second "back up to the surface" and no adviser.

**The ladder, timed from an empty inventory (nights skipped).** Food hunted and cooked by 10 minutes; wooden pickaxe, crafting table, furnace; stone pickaxe at 12 minutes; stone sword at 13; iron pickaxe at 15; shield at 17; iron sword at 19; bucket at 26. Every rung was announced as it started, Jev chose every source, and no adviser or generative model was involved. The phase is now "reach the Nether", which begins with full iron armour: twenty-four ingots, the first long stretch with no visible rung. Three fixes came out of watching: furnaces travel with the bot, placed workstations survive a restart, and two causes of repeated chat lines are gone (the repeat filter had been overwritten by mineflayer's chat plugin; a silent tunnel step had been resetting the narration key).

**Opportunities.**

**Run two reached the Nether at 2h52 (2026-09-21 22:32 UTC)** with the full iron set, golden boots on, diamond sword, shield, bucket, ten obsidian poured from one bucket, and the spawn point at a base with a chest. No deaths so far.

100. *Deaths five to seven, and a mistake of mine.* Five: burned by the blaze swarm after digging in beside the spawner room. Six: a hoglin; "Cornered. Holding here" because netherrack was not a building material, so with sixty in the pockets there was nothing to wall off with (Nether stone now is). Seven: a wither skeleton at the same room. Changes: a spawner's worth of blazes is fought from a bunker (src/bunker.js: a one-wide tunnel dug into the rock away from them, held with the shield up, blazes cut down at the door, rods collected when none has a line of sight); blaze sightings are remembered by place and the hunt walks back to the busiest one instead of sweeping from the portal; another of the hunted kind never ends a fight (the crowd rule counts kin in view, three is a swarm). And the mistake: a late monitor notice made me think the kit-restore aid had fired on a fresh connection's zero-health frame and duplicated the kit; I cleared the "duplicate" by console and stripped Jev's real kit in the Nether, then gave back exactly the twenty-one stacks the console reported removing. Net effect nil; labelled here. The aid now triggers on the death line only.
99. *The freeze, found.* Two inspector stacks from the supervisor: one deep in the dashboard trace's `clean`, one in the state file's `writeFileSync`, both under the main loop. Not one slow call but a spin: a step that returns without waiting on anything real (a synchronous throw swallowed by a handler) ran the loop thousands of passes a second, and with only microtask awaits the event loop never served I/O, so the server timed the player out and the process sat at 110% CPU. The loop now yields to I/O every pass, and more than twenty passes a second is logged with the step and slowed. The serialiser also got a node budget and a cycle guard.
98. *Standing beside the hole.* Back at the shelf the descent judged the drop from the bot's own column (a void beside last visit's shaft) and gave up; the face was shunned for ten minutes and the sweep wandered. A hole beside the bot with a safe landing is now stepped into.
97. *The portal entered at its top.* The entry aimed at whichever portal block was found first; the bot pillared a netherrack column beside the frame and stood with its head under the lintel, walking into obsidian. The lowest block of the frame is the target now, on the way back too.
96. *Fourth death: cornered by a wither skeleton, 4h33.* The blaze fight itself began (health seventeen after the first exchange), then a wither skeleton closed and "Cornered. Holding here" was the last line. Two changes: the shield comes up for the cooldown between sword swings (a wither skeleton hits from three blocks every second; unguarded swordplay loses the trade), and once a fortress has been walked it is patrolled rather than left, since blazes spawn as time passes. The kit-restore aid returned the pockets but not the worn armour: its equipment poll read the last forty console lines and the chat had pushed the equipment line out. Now four hundred, and this one set was put back by console, labelled.
95. *Scaffolding was dirt and cobblestone only.* The pathfinder pillars and bridges only with the blocks in its scaffolding list, and in the Nether the pockets hold netherrack; with the cobblestone spent on one span the bot could neither climb to the fortress fifteen blocks overhead nor cross to it, and the stalk of every blaze up there failed at once. Any plain stone carried now scaffolds. Within a minute of the restart Jev climbed from the lava shore to the fortress floor.
94. *The pathfinder's span.* The terrain read showed thirty-eight cobblestone in a one-wide line at y=64 with nothing solid below for twelve blocks: the pathfinder had been bridging toward the fortress across an open cavern, a block at a time with a forty-five-second search each, and the bot stood on the span under blaze fire (death three's fall). Across open air a span is now laid straight at the structure, a block ahead at a time, sneaking, up to sixty-four blocks. The drop onto a fortress roof may be nine blocks at full health.
93. *Reading the terrain from outside.* Most of tonight's guesses were wrong until the dashboard grew a local route (`/api/blocks?x=&y=&z=&r=`) listing the non-air blocks and the entities around the bot. The shelf, the span and the void under it were obvious in one call. Use it before theorising.
92. *The bot process froze for five minutes.* Right after "Tending the plot before bed" at 4h04 the event loop stopped: 110% CPU, the dashboard silent, the server timed the player out twenty seconds later, and the process needed a KILL. The cause is not found yet (the plot chores have no loop without an await; the pathfinder's synchronous search is the suspect). A supervisor now restarts the bot when the dashboard has not answered for a minute, after trying to capture the stuck stack with the inspector. Controlled-run aid, like the kit restore.
91. *Third death: shot off a ledge, a twenty-block fall, then lava at three health, 4h03.* Recovering health on the shelf above the fortress, under blaze fire from beyond the sixteen-block threat radius, the bot stood and lost what it was waiting to regain, and a fireball's knockback took it off the edge. Hurt in the last four seconds, a shooter in view now counts at thirty-two blocks, so the recovery moves out of the line of fire. The fall itself is not yet explained. The kit-restore aid missed this death: its death-line pattern expected `] Jev` where the console prints `]: Jev`; this kit was given back by console and is labelled as such, and the pattern is fixed.
90. *Second death: slain by a wither skeleton, twenty to nothing in six seconds, 3h58.* Walking a sweep leg through a fortress corridor at four blocks a second into two or three mobs. Two reasons it did not fight: the flee rule fought only mobs within 2.2 blocks, and a wither skeleton hits from three, so at two and a half the bot was searching for a route instead of swinging (now: within a sword's reach, 3.2 blocks or a strikeable target). And the blaze hunt itself was a flight: a stalked blaze at sixteen blocks tripped the threat check, so Jev fled the thing it was hunting ("I think I lost it"); the encounter's isolation rule counted every other blaze within twenty as a crowd, and every blaze in a fortress has another in view. Now: the stalk is an encounter, another blaze blocks a fight only within eight blocks, and mid-encounter a second mob interrupts only when nearly on the bot (eight for a shooter, five for melee).
89. *The sweep from a shelf.* With the fortress below and ignored, the sweep leg's staircase shuffled along the shelf toward a leg target ninety-six blocks off across an open cavern, fifty steps without gaining a block. A leg now walks with the pathfinder first (it bridges and climbs) and tunnels only where that finds no way; a direction that gives no ground for four attempts and twenty seconds turns the compass a quarter, said at most once a minute.
88. *A fortress under a shelf.* Nether bricks eight blocks straight down, a cave pocket between, and every tick tried the same approach: level goal, slanted goal, staircase, none of which can step off a ledge. Three fixes: closer counts and a shuffle does not when judging an approach; a face that will not be approached is shunned for ten minutes so the sweep meets the fortress elsewhere; and above the structure with a solid landing within five blocks, the bot digs the block underfoot and drops (never over lava, a longer fall, or below twelve health). With that Jev was inside the fortress within two minutes of the restart.
87. *First death of run two: burned by a blaze at six health, 3h45.* The first Nether trip went in with two steaks. After the first blaze exchange the bot stood at thirteen health and fifteen hunger, and at fifteen hunger there is no regeneration, so the wait for eighteen never ended; the blaze found it at six. Now: twenty-four food points before the crossing (the stock-driven search fills them at the base), fourteen health in full armour is enough to fight, and out of food off-world means back through the portal. The respawn was at the base bed beside the stash chest, as designed. The kit-restore aid missed the instant respawn on its five-second poll; it now triggers on the server's death line, and this one kit was given back by hand and is labelled as such.
86. *Frozen at the pocket by the fortress wall.* The dig-in pocket against the fortress wall reserved every stair cell within two blocks as construction, and the tunnel had no choice and the retreat no route: thirty-eight rounds. An emergency pocket is one night's stop, not construction, and its cells are not reserved.
85. *A blaze on the wall, twenty-nine blocks off.* The stalk stood in front of it for sixteen persistence rounds while the observed hunt, which looks within twenty-four, never saw it. A mob seen far off is closed on; one with no way to it is set aside and the fortress walk goes on.
84. *Rings around the portal do not find a fortress.* Fortress strips run north to south, so the blaze hunt now sweeps east or west in ninety-six-block legs at a safe height, turns for nether bricks in view, and once inside walks the nearest fresh stretch of the structure; the staircase may cut fortress and Nether blocks on the way.
83. *Digging in from harmless piglins.* With the golden boots on, piglins at eight blocks still counted as shooters and the bot dug in. A piglin leaves a player in gold alone; a brute does not, and a hit ends the truce.
82. *Crust out of reach, and the lake forgotten.* A crust block no dry spot could be found beside was retried every tick, and the step never poured again while any crust stood; chased off by a creeper the bot stood at its base sixty blocks from its own crust, and with no lava within forty-eight blocks would have started a new shaft. Crust that cannot be stood beside is set aside for five minutes, and the lake already found comes before a new shaft.
81. *A hundred and one blocks from bed.* A search for any water walked the bot five blocks past the bed rule at dusk and it dug in for the night. The base's pond is the first choice for water within reach; the bed counts as a walk out to a hundred and sixty blocks, started early when far.
80. *Home before bedtime, then back to work.* The go-home rule delivered the bot to the bed at 11229 and the work loop took the minute back and dived to the lava; the two traded every ten seconds until 12541. Home before bedtime is now a chore (stock the chest, harvest and bake, tend, breed), and once, the plot grows a column for the next day; the column is the evening chores' work, not the ladder's.
79. *The bed from twenty blocks.* A single approach goal failed from twenty blocks on the surface; the walk to the bed tries the stand cell, then two and three blocks from the foot.
78. *Retilled after every visit.* Walking and jumping across the plot turned its farmland back to dirt, and each return to the base began with the hoe. Farmland is a block the pathfinder walks around now; tilling and planting stand beside the cell anyway.
77. *Three ores, then the smelt.* With the vein rule in, three iron went into the pockets in five seconds and the rest of the vein sat at seven blocks: a six-block leash and a candidate list made before the first dig. The pool is re-read up to three times as the vein opens, on a ten-block leash from where the bot stands.
76. *One ore, then a walk.* The mine step returned after its first block and the plan re-ran, so a vein of eleven iron was taken one block per trip, and four raw iron lay a block from the bot while the router turned every pickup spot down. The step now works the nearest candidates on through the vein (ore to a stack; the stash keeps the surplus), a chosen source is followed as its neighbours come into view, and a drop within arm's reach is walked at directly: pickup is proximity, not a path.
75. *A finished base with five of nine cells.* Two plot cells had been trampled back to dirt and two sat under the first night's shell, and a completed base was never revisited. A base reopens when its plot is trampled or built on; blocked cells have their cover dug and shell cobblestone swapped for dirt, then are tilled and planted.
74. *"I'm safe inside", then "Morning" at night.* The pocket down the shaft was opened for a walk to the bed that failed, and the leave line was the morning line whatever the reason. A pocket is left for the bed only from the surface, a failed direct walk pauses the walk for two minutes but not the climb out, and leaving a shelter says why.
73. *The bed from the bottom of a mine.* At 12541 the bed was chosen from nineteen blocks down and the path search timed out. From dusk (11000) with a bed at home within a hundred blocks the bot heads home; underground it climbs out by its own stairs first, by the surface-return machinery, then walks; the bed is only offered from the surface.
72. *Five mobs in the cave, shaft frozen.* Every stair cell was ruled unsafe with mobs around and the shaft stood still for ten attempts. Rock is a wall to them too: when the safety rule empties the choices, the stairs go on in the direction that gains the most ground from the nearest mob. The staircase may also cut the bot's own dig-in cobblestone now.
71. *Up and down the stairs.* At dusk the survival layer decided on a shelter and climbed, was asked again at the top, chose to continue, and the work layer walked back down; the previous build had also recorded a good sleep as a failure and put the bed out of play for ten minutes. A shelter decision holds for two minutes like staying up; a sleep is confirmed by the clock.
70. *One iron per trip.* The iron for the armour came from a vein on the hillside above the base, one block per visit: the "finish the tree" rule only existed for logs, so after each ore the bot went back to its shaft, dug a few stairs, and climbed the hill again, fourteen times. A chosen source is now worked while blocks of it are within six and the step is short.
69. *"I'm working on it."* was the answer to "Jev status" for every step the dream added. Status now names the rung, the step (the staircase, the hunt, the levelling, the base chores) and where the base is.
68. *The shell that ate the base.* The first night at the finished base was spent in a shell built against the bed and the chest, and leaving it dug the chest as the door, then the bed. A shelter door is shell or rock, never a placed block, and the bed and chest steps clear leftover shell before placing. A bed within a walk now rules the shelter out altogether: the night question is sleep or stay up.
67. *Two blocks from the bed, walled in.* The sleep option knew only a carried bed, then only a bed in view; the bot spent one night sealed in beside its claimed bed and another thirty blocks from it. A bed remembered as claimed within a hundred blocks counts (later widened to a hundred and sixty, to take in the base's pond and plot), a sealed pocket within that walk is opened for it, and a sleep is confirmed by the clock jumping, not by a flag the server does not always set.
66. *Leaf litter.* The chest could not be placed and the plot could not be hoed: leaf litter on the cells. Ground cover is replaceable when placing, and cleared before hoeing.
65. *Sixty-three level cells.* Run two reached the home rung at fourteen minutes, and the site search failed three times in one second on a birch hill: the layout wanted a nine-by-seven footprint at one height, and the three attempts were three ticks in one spot, so the base was deferred half an hour. A site may now need a little levelling, up to forty-eight blocks of digging bumps and trees and laying dirt in dips, done as a home step before the bed; the scan takes one surface candidate per column over the whole radius and the flattest near site wins; and a failed look counts once per place, a minute apart, three places before a deferral.

**Run two (2026-09-21 19:22 UTC).** A fresh world (`natural-20260921-b`) and fresh bot state, keeping only the run ledger. The first run had become a bug harvest: a thousand blocks from either portal, a day of leftover shells, and a ladder rewritten underneath it. This run exercises the new order from the first spawn: bed by the first dusk, home and stash near spawn, the mine, then the Nether from a base. Nights are Jev's own; the kit restore on death is the one aid, labelled as before.

64. *A village walked past is a bed, a home and a larder.* Nothing in the code knew a village from a field: the bed rung hunted sheep with beds standing forty blocks off, the base was sited by the portal, and a hungry bot wandered for a chicken beside a farm of ripe wheat. Now a bell, three or more hay bales or a pair of villagers within forty-eight blocks is a village; the main loops look every thirtieth step or once the bot has moved thirty-two blocks, remember it on the goal beside the portals (one entry per village, counts refreshed on a second look) and say so once. The bed rung offers a remembered village with beds within two hundred blocks ahead of the wool hunt and digs one up (it drops itself; the village remembers one fewer, and a village reached three times without a bed waits twenty minutes); a village within that reach is the home base anchor, so the respawn bed and the stash chest go up among the houses; and the forage choices gain a village within a hundred and twenty blocks, read off the world when it is loaded and off memory when not, that takes the ripe wheat, carrots and potatoes with the seed put back, digs one hay bale when the farms are bare (nine wheat, three loaves), and bakes through the planner. Jev still picks the rung, the water and the food option. Trading is not on the ladder; a cleric sells ender pearls, and that is the note left in the code. Built ahead of the run reaching a village; not yet seen on the isolated server, so the first things to watch are the announcement on the first village and whether the bed comes up out of a villager's house without the villager in the doorway.

25. *Obsidian was going to be a dead end.* The ladder's Nether phase asks for ten obsidian, and the planner only knew how to mine a natural deposit, which the Overworld rarely offers within reach. A bucket of water emptied on the shore of a lava pool turns every source the flow touches into obsidian: code now finds the pool, the dry shore block to pour from and the crust blocks that are safe to open (nothing molten beside or beneath), and the plan requires the diamond pickaxe and a water bucket like any other tool. Checked on the isolated server with a built 3x3 pool: nine sources became nine obsidian in one pour, the source was scooped back, and the outflow pushed the bot three blocks out over the new crust, so it walks back before scooping. Built ahead of the run reaching it.
49. *Home before the mine.* Five of the six deaths in this run came on descents or in the Nether, and every one cost a climb from world spawn; every food reserve was a hunt of unknown length. The ladder now has a home rung after the bucket and before iron armour: a site chosen once per world on level ground beside water (near the first portal, else the house, else where the bot stands), a bed from sheep's wool used once so the respawn point moves home, a nine-cell wheat plot from a wooden hoe and the seeds that clearing grass drops (kept now, not tossed), and a gated pen for two cows led in with wheat. Tending, harvesting and baking, luring and breeding are idle chores Jev chooses between, and bread and steak at the base count as a reserve within reach for the food rules, so a hungry bot within a hundred and thirty blocks walks home instead of wandering for a chicken. Built ahead of the run reaching it; not yet seen on the isolated server, so the first thing to watch is the bed placement and the gate.
48. *A mining shaft's budget outlived the run.* The staircase counter for iron, 512 steps, is kept per resource across restarts; by the seventh climb it was spent and every iron dig refused with "budget exhausted", persistence and all. A spent budget now starts a fresh shaft from where the bot stands, like the surface search does. Fixed after this run.
**Controlled run from here (2026-09-21 ~17:10 UTC).** With eight deaths each costing a ninety-minute climb, the run switched to restoring the kit on death from the last inventory and equipment snapshot (a scratch script writing `give` and `item replace` to the server console), so the Nether, stronghold and End phases can be exercised at the rate they teach. Like the skipped nights, this is labelled here and is not acceptance evidence; a clean run follows once the long tail is worked through.

**Night skipping ended (2026-09-21 18:20 UTC).** From here nights are Jev's to handle: a bed is a rung after the stone sword, carried rather than left at home, and at bedtime the survival question is sleep, shelter, or stay up. The kit restore on death is the one controlled-run aid still running.

63. *A chest of things for later, and eyes open on the way.* Every death cost the wool, string, feathers and iron gathered since the last one, and every rung that needed them hunted again; the same bot walked past string on the ground and sheep at the fence because nothing on the ladder wanted them at that minute. The stash chest now keeps a second class of contents beside the kit, keepsakes: wool over three, all string, feathers, bones, gunpowder, leather and obsidian, arrows over a quiver, flint and iron over a tool's worth, gold beyond the boots until the boots exist, diamonds once the diamond pickaxe exists, logs over eight, coal over sixteen and seeds and crops over a stack (pearls, rods and powder keep what the eyes need, since the ladder counts them in the pockets). They go in with the kit whenever Jev chooses to stock the chest, and they come out when a rung wants them: the bed rung asks for its wool, and any acquire rung's plan is read backwards against the chest, so ingots in the chest answer the sword's craft and the mine and smelt beneath it are never walked. On the way, a keepsake or a piece of the kit lying within eight blocks is picked up by rule during a mine or tunnel step, ten seconds and back (never what the surplus tidy would toss again), and a lone sheep within twelve blocks with fewer than three wool carried, or a chicken with fewer than four feathers, is offered to Jev every third step beside continuing, the chase going through the bed rung's sheep hunt or the bow rung's mob hunt and returning to the step. Not yet exercised on the server.
62. *A daylight search for a spider.* The bow rung sent the hunt to the exploration frontier by day: seven hundred blocks in four minutes, ending in the sea, while the home site went unbuilt behind it. Spiders are a night thing on the surface and a cave thing by day. The bow and arrows are night rungs now (or a spider in view, or string in hand); by day the ladder goes on to a diamond sword, which is deep work where the spiders are anyway, and a hunt circles its start in rings rather than walking the frontier.
61. *A bucket is a pond.* The home site wanted natural water within sixty-four blocks and failed four times on a mountain top while a full bucket sat in the pockets. With no pond in reach and a water bucket carried, the site is level ground anywhere near the anchor, the water cell is a ground block the bot opens and fills once before tilling, and the farmland is hydrated by it like any shore.
60. *A bed instead of a skipped night.* Walled in and waiting was the largest share of the run's standing still, and a night slept passes in a hundred ticks. The bed is a rung after the stone sword (three wool from a sheep, three planks; a sheepless search is set aside for twenty minutes), it is carried, and at 12541 the survival choice becomes sleep in the bed here, or stay up two minutes at a time when armed and armoured and the dark is what the request needs, as a string hunt does. A sleep the server refuses hands that night to the shelter path. Jev chooses; the site, the placement, the pickup at dawn and the bedtime are code.
59. *Coal walked past.* With no fuel in the pockets and coal ore in the shaft wall, the smelts burned the planks the next pickaxe needed, and the only check for ore along the way ran after mine steps, never in the tunnel, and asked Jev about "surplus", who chose to continue. Fuel is a rule now: coal within reach while fewer than eight units are carried is taken without a question, and the check runs from every tunnel step.
58. *Sealed in at the surface by its own lid.* At the top of the hand-dug climb the bot stood on grass under two blocks of cobblestone from an earlier night's shell, and the surface observer counted the lid as terrain: twenty minutes of "I can't reach a safe spot" while the route search looked for a way up that no stair choice could dig, since cobblestone is not natural stone. A thin lid of shelter material with sky above it is now dug straight up before any route search.
57. *Shoot back.* Most of the deaths in this run were arrows: skeletons in the caves, a crossbow piglin at five blocks, and a bot that could only answer at arm's length, so every shooter in view was a reason to run, and running is when the arrows land. The End fight already had a bow: the arc solved from the eye with lead and drop, the release waited on the rotation packet, the arrow confirmed by entity and inventory. That code now serves the whole run. Two rungs after the golden boots, a bow and a quiver of sixteen, whose ingredients are two new hunts: a spider for string (neutral by day, ranked below a cobweb in view and above a cobweb search; tripwire, which only exists where a player laid it, is no longer a string source) and a chicken for feathers, a passive chase with whatever is carried, no armour, no shield, no isolation from the flock. Feathers had no source at all before, so the End's own arrow supply could never have been planned. Under fire with a shooter in clear view at four to twenty blocks and an arc that is clear, code lists the shots, the retreat and, with blocks in the pack, the pocket, and Jev picks; without Jev the bot shoots while health holds and retreats otherwise. A melee mob at arm's length keeps the swing rule, and nothing draws a bow with a zombie three blocks off. After a release the shield comes up until the next look. A blaze hunt shoots while the blaze is in view and closes with the sword when no arrow can reach it. Built ahead of the run reaching it; not yet seen on the isolated server, so the first things to watch are the chicken chase and the first arrow at a skeleton.
56. *A pickaxe that breaks in the shaft.* The stone pickaxe had seventy-seven uses at the top of the iron tunnel and the shield rung had just spent the planks; it broke at y=37 and the climb out was by hand, seven seconds a block, through two nights. The trip check only ran when wood was short, and nothing watched the tool once the digging started. Now every step checks the best pickaxe: under twenty-four uses with no sound spare, and cobblestone and sticks (or planks, or a log) in the pockets, the spare is crafted on the spot before the current step continues.
55. *The ladder went dead after the first Nether entry.* The game stage only consulted the preparation ladder while the Nether milestone was unset, so every climb after the first went straight to "reach the Nether": armour came back only because the combat check demands it, and the iron pickaxe, bucket, home and golden boots rungs never ran again. Three Nether trips went in with less than the first one. The gate is now "no Nether supplies carried", and a full kit passes the ladder in one call.
54. *A spare kit beside the bed.* Every death in this run (36 to 52) cost the whole kit and then the same hour of gathering from a wooden pickaxe; the bed moved the respawn home but the respawn still started with nothing. The home rung now places a chest beside the foot of the bed as soon as the bed is claimed, and the base state remembers it with its contents as of the last look inside. The kit is a pickaxe, a sword, eight logs, a stack of cobblestone, eight cooked food, a crafting table, a furnace and a water bucket when a second is carried; "stock the stash" is an idle chore Jev chooses between the farm ones (the best tool of each kind stays in hand, the next best goes in; wood, stone and food go in beyond what a descent keeps), and before a Nether crossing the ladder leaves diamonds, gold, spare ingots and raw ore at home when the base is within reach. On a respawn at the bed, or within reach of the base with an empty kit, a restock rung runs ahead of the preparation rungs and takes out what the pockets are short of plus whatever the next rung was about to gather. Tools move by slot so the spare goes in and the kept one stays; the moves are worked out again against the real contents once the lid is open, and a chest found missing reopens the home rung. Built ahead of the run reaching it; not yet seen on the isolated server, so the first things to watch are the chest placement beside the bed and the first restock after a death.
53. *Gold on the feet.* Piglins ignore a player wearing any gold; golden boots are a rung after the armour set, and the feet slot prefers them when carried, at the cost of one armour point. Not yet exercised.
52. *Eighth death: dug in, then dug straight back out.* A skeleton at seven blocks, no escape footing; the new dig-in closed the sides once, the next loop saw no shelter and the tunnel step dug back out into the arrows, then the charge rule walked toward the skeleton at eight health. Now, shot at with no way out, the bot builds the full shell where it stands, registers it as tonight's pocket, and the next loop finds itself inside a sealed shelter and waits until nothing is watching; charging needs twelve health. Fixed after this run. Climb nine.
51. *Seventh death: shot by a crossbow piglin while holding.* Third Nether visit. Piglins now counted as hostile, the bot walled off twice and fought one at arm's length, but a crossbow piglin at five blocks with no escape footing in reach met the hold rule, which stands still, and took half its health before the charge rule (close the gap when armed and able) could be deployed. Also noted: one piece of gold armour makes piglins ignore a player; golden boots cost four ingots and would remove the encounter class entirely. Worth a rung before the Nether. Climb eight.
50. *Stale pockets and an outrun shaft.* Nineteen dug-in pockets from earlier nights were still registered and reserving their shells; the staircase toward the portal could dig nothing but the two floor cells of the pocket it had just left and bounced between them. A dug-in shelter is now forgotten on leaving. Then the tunnel routine rejoined a worksite thirty blocks behind the bot when it was twelve from the portal; a worksite well behind the bot for the same target is replaced by where it stands. And with the pickaxe broken outright, the planner had no stone stair to offer; the portal tunnel now makes a pickaxe first.
49. *The walk to an underground portal.* A portal at y=-11 is on no surface route; the sixty-second walk ended short forty-seven times. Within forty-eight blocks the bot walks; otherwise, or when the walk fails, it digs toward the portal the way it reaches an ore.
47. *A food reserve is not worth the whole day.* Respawned on a bare mountain at full hunger, the seventh climb spent half an hour in "search food" for a reserve it did not yet need, thirty persistence attempts and one-minute pauses between, because a stock flag from an earlier hunt never cleared. A stock-driven search that finds nothing in five minutes is now set aside for twenty; real hunger still forages. Fixed after this run.
46. *Sixth death: shot during a back-off.* An iron dig ran out its staircase budget, persistence kicked in, and its pause, sixty seconds by then, watched for cancellation and air but not for mobs; a skeleton walked up over fifty-five seconds and shot the bot standing still. The pause and the shake-loose moves now check for threats every hundred milliseconds and hand a threat to the survival layer. Fixed after this run. Climb seven.
45. *Fifth death: drowned digging a lake floor.* Walking back to the portal the route crossed a lake, the pathfinder stalled, and the shake-loose recovery, which digs under the feet as a last resort, dug the lake bed by hand with the bot underwater: a half-minute dig with no air check inside it. Shake loose now swims up when submerged and never digs there, and every recovery dig runs under the air guard. Fixed after this run. Climb six.
44. *Fourth death: the Nether's mobs were not on the list.* Second time through the portal, straight to the blaze hunt and then, mid-search, slain by a magma cube in two seconds without a flee or a swing: magma cubes, slimes, ghasts, piglins and a few others were not in the hostile-mob set, so the danger layer never saw them. Added. Fixed after this run. Two Nether visits, two lessons, and the fortress search proper has not started yet.
43. *Third death: six zombies in a tunnel, and a bot placing blocks against them.* Walking back to its portal at y=-9 the bot met a pack of zombies; with a prepared pocket three blocks away and stone in hand the emergency branch chose to seal rather than fight, every placement was refused because a zombie stood in the cell, and health went from twenty to one in the loop. Now a mob at arm's length rules out sealing, and an armed bot at eight health or more fights it swing after swing without a route search in between (an unarmed or badly hurt bot still retreats). Fixed after this run. The kit was lost a third time, but the portals are remembered, so the way back is the ladder to flint and steel, not obsidian again.
42. *A food trip forgot the portal.* After the first crossing the food rule (stock, not hunger) sent the bot back through and two hundred and fifty blocks across the surface for a chicken; with no portal loaded within sixty-four blocks the Nether rung began planning a second portal, obsidian and all. Portals used are now remembered per dimension and walked back to, an unloaded frame counts as a portal elsewhere, and off the Overworld only real hunger sends the bot back.
41. *The Nether has no surface.* Through the portal at last, the first thing the bot did was start a food search, which climbs to "the surface" first; in the Nether that meant digging up toward the bedrock roof three times a second. Off the Overworld, surface return is now a no-op, the hunger trigger drops to real hunger (8), and the only food option is to go back through the portal. Fixed after this run.
40. *Ten obsidian in one pour, then a portal on deepslate.* Third time down, properly supplied, the pour converted the lake edge, the crust was mined block by block with the bucket scooped back in between, and the frame went up beside the lake once the site rule accepted deepslate underfoot. The portal lit and the bot crossed over about seven hours after the dream was given, two deaths included.
39. *Second death: knocked into the lava at the pouring spot.* Six hours in, with the diamond pickaxe crafted underground and the bucket already full, the bot reached a lava lake at y=-54, announced the pour, was cornered by a mob at the edge and "tried to swim in lava". Everything burned. The escape logic chose footing by distance from the mob and never looked at lava; the hold branch stood its ground with lava a block away. Now: footing beside lava is never an escape, a bot with lava within two blocks moves off the edge before anything else, and the obsidian step waits while a mob is in view. Fixed after this run; the ladder restarted from a wooden pickaxe a second time.
38. *Eleven centimetres of body in the cell.* Sealing a pocket, the bot stood at x=-506.81 and asked the server to fill the cell at x=-508; its 0.6-wide body overlapped that cell and every placement was refused with "the block is still air", eleven times a minute until persistence gave up on the site. Placement now checks the bot's own box against the target cell and steps back out of it first. Fixed after this run.
37. *Buried is not "rock within twelve blocks".* Standing on a diamond ore at y=14 with a cave shaft open above, the shelter rule saw twelve blocks of air and demanded a two-block exit; there was none, so no site. Buried now means anything solid between the site and the sky. And the diamond itself was set aside because digging beneath the feet was refused outright; a one-block drop onto solid ground is now allowed. Both fixed after this run.
36. *Died in iron armour without a swing.* Tunnelling for lava at y=-16 the bot met a zombie and a skeleton in its own staircase, reported "cornered, holding here and defending" fifty-seven times in thirty seconds and never attacked once: the strike check wants a clear ray from the eye to the mob's upper body, and on a staircase the mob a step up or down is hidden behind the stair edge while it lands every hit. It died with the diamond pickaxe, the water bucket, full iron armour and the sword, two hundred blocks from spawn, and the ladder restarted from a wooden pickaxe. A mob within two blocks is now struck whatever the ray says. Fixed after this run. And when cornered with stone in hand and the mob still a cell away, the bot now walls that cell shut, two blocks high, instead of holding; a mob already in the cell is fought.
34. *Diamonds and a diamond pickaxe, four hours in.* Once the pockets had room the iron rung took twelve minutes, the diamond dig found three at y=-2, and the diamond pickaxe was crafted. The obsidian plan then sent the bot back up for a water bucket; the bucket now comes before the diamond dig in the plan so it is filled on the surface on the way down.
35. *A shelter can fail verification forever over one buried cell.* One shell cell was an air pocket with solid blocks on all six sides; the builder forgives such a cell as complete and never fills it, the shelter check counted it as missing, and the seal step threw five times a second until persistence kicked in. An enclosed pocket now counts as sealed. Fixed after this run.
33. *Full pockets are a silent stall.* By the third hour the bot carried twelve stacks of cobblestone, two of diorite, one of andesite and assorted gravel, dirt and copper. With no free slot, mined iron ore lay where it dropped ("I can't get at the iron ore here"), the crafting grid spilled its contents when a craft failed, and seven furnaces accumulated. Nothing in the code ever dropped anything. Surplus over a per-item cap (three stacks of cobblestone, none of the granites, two of each workstation, a little dirt and gravel) is now tossed whenever fewer than four slots are free, biggest surplus first, with one line of chat. Fixed after this run.
32. *A skeleton in the next cave vetoed the staircase.* Every stair step is checked against hostiles within sixty-four blocks, and a ranged mob keeps a twenty-block ring; a skeleton twenty blocks away through solid rock made every step toward it unsafe, and the bot stood in its pocket for another half hour. A mob with no line of sight now counts only within six blocks. Fixed after this run.
31. *A handler failing inside the loop's catch ended the request.* Moving on from a resource threw from inside runGoal's catch ("no dry route away from the blocked staircase"), the exception left runGoal, and the dream request was parked with "say resume"; the dream relaunched it, so the run survived by luck. Every handler that runs inside a catch is now guarded. Fixed after this run.
30. *And then the pocket had no door.* Leaving a shelter looked for a two-block exit, which the staircase pocket never had, so after the dawn fix the bot waited with the reason "threats block the exits" instead. A pocket is now left through the closure the bot placed, preferring the side with the staircase behind it, and only mobs that can see in count. Fixed after this run.
29. *Dawn underground never came.* Sealed in a pocket at y=18, the bot waited twenty-two minutes past daybreak because two skeletons and a creeper wandered the caves within twenty blocks, behind solid rock. Underground there is always a mob somewhere; the wait now holds only for one that can see in or is at the wall. Fixed after this run.
28. *Three pickaxes at six durability and no wood is a sealed shaft.* The expedition prep ran once, long ago, and never again; the bot went down for iron with worn tools, the tunnel and the surface exit both demanded a pickaxe with eight durability left, the only fix was wood, and the wood was on the surface. Two changes: the exit is now dug for the way out rather than the drops, so a worn pickaxe is used until it breaks and bare hands finish the climb (slow, but finite); and a pickaxe under 24 durability with no wood carried reopens the expedition prep before any descent, which now asks for a spare pickaxe and four logs of whatever tree was seen. Fixed after this run.
27. *A Jev outage froze the ladder.* TypeSafe returned 503 for stretches of several minutes during this run (eight of eight probes failed at one point), and every survival decision at dusk or on hunger threw, so the bot sat in its retry loop with a growing backoff while the rungs waited. The decision tree is now walked with a code default when the service is unreachable (shelter before food before the request, otherwise the first option), the record says so, the Observatory colours it, and the bot announces the outage once and its end once. Cancellations and rejected requests still throw; only outages fall back. Fixed after this run.
26. *Hunger underground is not the same as hunger on the surface.* With no cooked reserve, the bot went hunting whenever hunger dipped to 18 of 20, which underground meant abandoning the iron shaft, climbing forty blocks and walking the surface for a chicken, then digging back down; three such trips in one hour. The trigger now stays at 18 on the surface, where the animals are, and drops to 12 underground. Fixed after this run.
24. *Burn the coal you carry.* The furnace only ever burned planks, so with two coal in its pockets the bot left its iron shaft, climbed to the surface and set off for one oak log to cook a chicken. Coal, charcoal, coal blocks, kelp blocks and blaze rods are now fuel when enough is carried for the batch, and a saved batch burns whatever is on hand. Fixed after this run; the trip it saves is the commonest one in the log.
23. *Spend the cheap tool.* The iron pickaxe broke after twenty minutes because every block, including two hundred cobblestone, was dug with the best tool carried. A stone pickaxe harvests stone and iron ore; iron is only needed from diamonds up. The bot now digs with the lowest tier that harvests the block and keeps the iron for what needs it. Fixed after this run; the ladder had already healed itself by mining and smelting more iron for a replacement.

22. *Armour is a rung too.* "Reach the Nether" hides twenty-four ingots of work behind one label; a helmet, a chestplate, leggings and boots are four announced rungs, and Jev could be asked whether to go for armour first or scout for a lava lake and obsidian while the iron accumulates.

20. *Take the furnace with you.* Crafting tables placed by the session are collected on leaving; furnaces are not, so a bot that cooks in three places builds three furnaces. Pick it up when the smelt is done and the bot moves on, or remember where it stands and go back.
21. *The first rung should be the first line.* "Chasing my dream to beat the game: beat the game" said nothing; it now names the first rung. The win objective's own expedition prep (food reserve, tools) runs before the ladder, so the first visible rung is a food hunt rather than the stone pickaxe the chat promised; the prep and the ladder should be one list.

## muarczp0 · 2026-09-21 · "build a house" + 2 cycles · controlled (nights skipped) · PASS

**Result.** House built and verified at tick 5678 of the run, then two full day/night cycles survived (48,000 world ticks) with no deaths, no recovery escalation and no generative call. Nights were skipped from the console while the bot was sealed in, so this is a controlled run, not acceptance evidence; the bot's own behaviour was untouched. The first complete run of the evening.

**What happened.** Trials five and six were cut short by the same creeper: it followed the bot from spawn and interrupted it sixteen times in ten minutes, because the escape put six blocks between them and a creeper does not burn off at dawn. The escape from a persistent mob now reaches past its follow range, with the ordinary hop as fallback, and the chat says an escape once and "I think I lost it" after. Trial seven then built and verified the house with no escapes at all, hunted a cow for beef on the way, sealed itself into the house at dusk, and after the night was skipped came back out to... stand there. Shelter and food were sufficient, so the only survival option was to wait.

**Opportunities.**

17. *Idle time is ambition time.* With nothing asked and nothing urgent, code now lists the feasible chores (cook the raw beef, make stone tools, stock wood, torches from carried coal) and the next rung of the beat-the-game ladder, and Jev chooses, with resting always on offer. Done after this run; the first idle rung of the ladder is "reach the Nether", which begins with combat supplies, so the finer rungs (iron pickaxe, shield, a bed) should become their own candidates so the climb is visible on screen within minutes rather than hours.
19. *The dream is live.* On the Creative play world the bot, given "your dream is to build a village", chose a stone cottage from the schematic shelf (45 validated designs, none generated at run time), sited it beside the standing buildings and built it, announcing the village score as it went. The Survival version ("your dream is to beat the game") climbs the finer ladder; its first rung is a stone pickaxe.

18. *Auto mode is a reframing, not a rewrite.* The `win` objective is already a hill-climber over observed milestones, and the idle loop already runs when nothing is queued. A standing ambition, per world, that the idle loop hands to the request runner as if a player had typed it, with Jev choosing the next milestone from what code enumerates, is the natural next step, and the Creative village is the first demo to build it against since every milestone there is buildable today.

## muar64lo · 2026-09-21 · "build a house" + 2 cycles · controlled (nights skipped) · in progress

**So far.** First run with the never-give-up loop. A creeper followed the bot from spawn: four escapes in the first thirty steps for four logs.

**Opportunities.**

16. *A short hop does not shake a creeper.* Creepers do not burn in daylight and follow to about sixteen blocks; the flee puts six blocks between them and the bot goes back to the same tree, so the same creeper interrupts again a minute later. Sprint past the follow range, or move the work to a source on the far side of it, before resuming. Whether to relocate the work or just outrun it is a fair question for Jev; the distance is a rule.

## muaqvemv · 2026-09-21 · "build a house" + 2 cycles · controlled (nights skipped) · FAIL at the house, by a rule that has since been removed

**What happened.** Started at day (the console set it). Narration was live for the first time: "Getting oak log from the oak log 8 blocks away", "Crafting 96 oak planks", "Clearing the site", two creeper escapes. Clearing the site hit stone with no pickaxe; after three failures Jev's recovery pick chose "gather a stone pickaxe" on its own, at 0.6+ confidence, the bot got cobblestone, crafted the pickaxe and went back ("Okay, back to your request"). No generative adviser involved. Then the house tree came up empty: every placement on the lowest layer had failed once in the last two minutes, the two-minute suppression excluded all of them, and `No feasible house action remains` was a terminal Blocked. Run over with 97 planks and two pickaxes in hand.

**Opportunities.**

13. *Suppression is a preference, not a veto.* When every candidate has failed recently, offer them all again rather than nothing. Fixed.
14. *Nothing should be terminal except the player, the game, or an impossible ask.* The request loop no longer has a failure budget or a step budget: after retries, Jev's recovery pick and moving on, it says so once, shakes itself loose (soft blocks, then natural walls, then the floor onto a safe landing), leaves a failing resource for elsewhere, backs off for a growing pause up to a minute, and goes again. Idle survival does the same. Only definitional impossibilities (bedrock in Survival, a mob that does not spawn in Peaceful) are explained and parked. Done after this run.
15. *Per-tree narration is noise.* "Getting oak log from the oak log 8 blocks away" repeated for every trunk. A resource is now announced once with its count; the crafting, building and survival lines stay.

## muapyq2c · 2026-09-21 · "build a house" + 2 cycles · house PASS, endurance FAIL at 22,720 of 48,000 ticks

**So far.** Dusk arrived a minute in. Jev chose `secure_shelter` over the house at 0.9+ confidence, crafted twelve planks, gathered dirt and sealed itself in. No errors, no recovery, no OpenRouter call. Then it waited.

**Opportunities.**

7. *Sleep through the night instead of waiting it out.* A night is nine and a half real minutes of nothing. A bed is three wool and three planks, and there were sheep at spawn. Whether to dig in or to fetch wool and sleep is a Jev trade-off (sheep distance, daylight left, hunger, how hurried the player sounded), and sleeping also resets the spawn point beside the shelter, which helps death recovery. Spectators are excluded from the sleep check.

   What it takes, in order: (a) a passive-mob handler in `mob-policy.js` so sheep count as a wool source without the full iron armour and shield the blaze and enderman encounters demand, and a colour check so only white sheep are hunted for a white bed; (b) two-cell oriented bed placement in the executor, since a bed is not in the buildable palette; (c) a `sleep_in_bed` survival option using mineflayer's `bot.sleep`, guarded by its own monster-nearby rule, with wake handling and the shelter fallback if sleep is refused; (d) a `make_a_bed` option at dusk when white sheep are observed within reach and daylight remains. Roughly a day of work with tests; the survival tree and the source machinery it plugs into already exist.

8. *A house needs a bed anyway.* Once (b) and (c) exist, the compact house should get a bed inside it, which makes every later night at home a five-second affair and sets the respawn point at the house.

**Later in the run.** Dawn at 04:13; Jev left the shelter, resumed logs, hit one unreachable tree, set it aside and moved to the next without any adviser (the new escape hatch), reached 25 logs, crafted 96 planks, built the house and verified it at tick 5678. Then idle survival wanted a food reserve (Normal asks for twelve points before night, though hunger was full), Jev chose `obtain_food → search_food`, and the bot wandered 75 blocks from the house and 30 blocks down to the shore. There it stood under a two-block gravel lip, which the surface observer reads as underground. No walking route to a "surface" landing was accepted, the staircase excavation demanded a pickaxe, the pickaxe needed wood, and wood is a surface resource: `Cannot excavate a surface exit`. Jev's recovery pick and then the generative adviser both chose "return to the surface", which failed the same way. Five failures, blocked. Never hurt, never hungry.

**Opportunities.**

9. *A hand-diggable overhang needs no pickaxe.* Gravel, dirt and sand come away in the hand; the tool rule should look at what is actually overhead before bootstrapping a pickaxe. Fixed after this run.
10. *"Underground" is too coarse near cliffs and shores.* A block under a lip on a beach is not a cave. The observer could look for sky within a few blocks sideways before treating the spot as enclosed, and the route search for a landing could allow a short swim.
11. *Food searches should not walk the bot off a cliff and out of sight of home.* Foraging with a full stomach and no food reserve is reasonable, but the search wandered 75 blocks and 30 down; bounding it to the surface around the house, or making distance part of what Jev weighs, would keep the bot where it can shelter.
12. *The runner's failure budget hides the good half.* This run completed the useful request cleanly; a single survival dead end ended it. Reporting the house PASS and the endurance FAIL separately, as the runner now logs them, is right; the roadmap should say the same.


## muappqyo · 2026-09-21 · "build a house" + 2 cycles · FAIL after 2.5 minutes

**What happened.** Fresh spawn at (-520, 93, 176) in an oak forest. Jev chose between two log sources at every step (335 to 425 ms a call, 0.9+ confidence) and gathered six logs from four trees, moving along a hillside. It reserved a house site at (-543, 93, 180) and then went for the oak tree directly beside it, whose trunk sits on a ledge two blocks up with leaves over the approach. `approachDryMining` found no standing position for any block of that tree. After three failures recovery ran: Jev answered *none* at 0.39 (right: the offered options were footings within three blocks and a dirt pile), the generative adviser proposed relocating and gathering twelve dirt, the dirt was gathered, the relocation route was gone by the time it ran, and the next attempt hit the same tree. Blocked, run over. Never died, never hungry.

**Opportunities.**

1. *A single unreachable tree must never end a request.* The plain error from the approach became the goal's terminal `Blocked`. It should mark that tree unreachable for a while and move on to the next source, and when no source is left nearby, explore for another tree. The world had dozens.
2. *Failure suppression was defeated by its own keys.* A source is keyed by its nearest block; once that key was marked failed, the same tree came back under its next-nearest block as a "new" source, six times in a row. Suppression has to match the whole cluster, not the seed block.
3. *Recovery needs an option that changes the situation.* Every offered footing was within three blocks of where the bot already stood. "Leave this area and look for the resource elsewhere" was the right answer and it was not on the list, so Jev said none and the expensive adviser was asked for nothing better.
4. *Reserving the house site next to the unreachable tree* made the later tree choice worse: the reserved cells are excluded from mining, narrowing an already awkward approach. Site selection could prefer spots not adjacent to standing trees, or reserve later.
5. *One Jev call per block.* Every step re-asked which source to work, even mid-tree. Committing to a chosen source until it is exhausted or fails would cut calls by most of an order of magnitude on gathering and read more like intent.
6. *Only-option decisions dominate the trail.* Eleven of twenty-seven decisions had a single source and cost no call. That is correct, and the Observatory shows it, but it means the interesting Jev choices are the ones between two trees a few blocks apart; the more valuable judgment (keep gathering here versus walk to that grove) is not yet a question.

**Jev versus OpenRouter in this run.** All 27 routing and action decisions were Jev. OpenRouter was asked once, for recovery, after Jev declined to pick. Without an OpenRouter key this run would have ended at the same place a minute sooner.
110. *mid-87-a, a vein followed straight down.* The user saw Jev digging straight down, more than they expected. Counted over the midgame trials: a hundred and ten descents of three blocks or more in one column, most of them the staircase stepping back and forth in a 1x2 footprint toward a target below, each step onto a floor it looked at and never dug. The real one was the night mine: an ore within reach was dug wherever it was, under the feet included, and at minute 90 a vein went from y 28 to y 24 one block and one drop at a time. The dig helper let any dig take the floor out from under the bot when the block below was solid, and looked below but not beside, where lava would run in. Now the floor is dug from a cell beside, as a player does; only a dig that inspected the column first (the shaft pocket, Jev's own "dig down" when stuck) drops into it, and not with lava beside.
111. *mid-87-a and mid-100-c, fifty minutes each on the stairs by hand.* Both midgame trials spent more time in ascend_to_surface than in everything else together, climbing at two blocks a minute with no pickaxe and no wood. In mid-87-a the night mine wore two iron pickaxes, five hundred uses, in twenty minutes, stopped at the twenty-four it kept for the way out whatever the depth, and ran out forty blocks down. The uses kept now grow with the rock over the head, two digs a block and a margin, and the night mine's option tells Jev how many uses the pickaxe has and how many are the climb. The other half, one stick short every time, and armour and a cave chest spending the last iron and wood, went to the decision audit as facts.
112. *mid-87-a, walled in by its own shelter.* Forty blocks down at dusk Jev built a pocket and it was saved as a shelter to use again; saved shelters are kept from every dig within two blocks, as construction is. In the morning the way up had "a building in the way" in every direction, the staircase was set aside toward target after target, and the audit called the loop at minute 130. A saved shelter is still kept from other work, but the bot standing in one digs out through its shell; a house is never dug through.
