113. *mid-87-b, no place for a table in its own pocket.* Six minutes in, sealed into a pocket at y 3 that was saved as a shelter, Jev needed a crafting table for its first pickaxe and found "no place" two hundred times: the rule that cuts a notch in the wall for a station, made for the foot of a shaft, left out the walls of a saved shelter as construction. The bot standing in a shelter cuts the notch in its own wall now; the table fills it, so the shell stays whole.

114. *mid-83-b, "drop nothing", again.* With seventeen dripstone, five pointed dripstone and a mushroom in full pockets, making room for a stone pickaxe came back "none" three times in three, replayed against Jev with the same pockets: the junk stacks split the vote (0.15, 0.14, 0.05) and "none" won at 0.24. Whether to drop anything and which stack are two questions now, asked together; replayed, "drop" took 0.87 to 0.91 and the pointed dripstone went every time. The drop option says when none of the stacks is without a use, and "none" says when the thing the room is for has none. A replay with only gear carried and a dripstone block to pick up still dropped the water bucket two times in three: a quirk of Jev's with that stack, noted, not ruled out.
115. *mid-83-b, burned at the edge of a flow.* Making obsidian at y -23, a skeleton came, Jev fought it beside the pool, and its body leaned into the edge of flowing lava. The lava test looked at the one cell under the middle of the feet, and the physics' own flag shrinks the body before it looks, so for three and a half seconds the survival layer defended and stepped out of the water while the health went from 18 to 2.7, two every half second. Leaving the lava began then, eating at 1.7, and it died. Lava now counts anywhere the body's box touches, at any level, as the game counts it, and the douse is not tried while standing in it.
116. *mid-100-c, two falls on the way home, then a third. Open.* At minute 151, walking home in a cave at full health, the route over a flooded shaft with the bot's own dirt scaffolding in it dropped it from y 12 to y 5 (20 to 16 health) and on to y -4 (to 9). The first fall is the one to explain: the pathfinder's drops are held to three blocks, and a gap jump over a hole that fails is a longer one. Not reproduced yet; not changed.

The drop is measured and said on every stance now, and a drop within two blocks that costs half the health or more, or ends in lava, is the edge the bot steps back from with a mob close, to ground three blocks from it, as it already did beside a drop in the next cell.
118. *mid-110-g, the first to the Nether, and a hoglin's toss.* Minute 99, the Nether reached; five minutes later on a ledge at y 70 with a hoglin about, the step back from the edge stalled twice, Jev chose to pillar and then to fight, and the toss put it twenty-six blocks down at full health. "Search for animals" failed at once, "no reachable surveyed ground", and was chosen again, three hundred and eighty-five times in eight minutes, until the audit called the loop. A search that finds nowhere to walk rests two minutes, so the other ways are chosen.

Five trials on this world have each died early, each for a different reason, all fixed in turn; 25583 moves to another world.
123. *mid-87-c, off a cliff at full health, toward a herd.* Climbing out by hand at y 60, Jev chose to walk back to a herd seen earlier; two seconds into the route it went down a twenty-five-block drop from the cliff-top, twenty health to none. The route cut diagonally past the corner of the drop: the pathfinder checks the corner cells of a diagonal for room for the body and, here, for lava and water, but not for a floor, and the body's width over the corner with a little drift is a fall. A diagonal is not taken past a corner with nothing to stand on within four blocks down; cardinal steps go round. This may be note 116's first fall too.

The skeleton hunt does not exempt a creeper, so the creeper most likely did not count as in sight (it had just pillared up, the creeper at a block's edge below); the flight record does not hold the mobs, so this is the likely reading, not a proven one. A creeper within four blocks counts now whether in sight or not, for the threat check and for the answer to it.
125. *mid-110-h, skeletons at 4.5 health.* Stuck in a block on the way up (twenty health to twelve in four seconds, dug out), then two skeletons: a bunker dug under fire, and at 4.5 health a fight and a charge, told only "hands back after six health lost". The fight options now say how few hits the health left is when it is under fourteen ("3 arrows from the skeleton, about 1.6 each after armour, end it"). Replayed, Jev still charged, 0.48 to 0.56: it follows the fight estimate, which counted 3.2 damage in nine seconds for both skeletons. The damage a second was about right (0.26 a second measured over the fifty-four seconds); the time was not: the estimate assumes the shooters are reached and struck, and here the charges kept failing on the ground between. The estimate needs the time to reach a shooter, and a charge that cannot get there, in it. Open.
126. *Note 125's open end: the charge that cannot get there.* The charge runs straight at a shooter a cell at a time over firm steps; that line is walked ahead now when the options are made, and the charge and the fight say whether the ground carries a run there or stops it short, and where. Replayed at 4.5 health against two skeletons: with the hits left alone, charge 0.48 to 0.56; with the ground said on the charge, charge 0.13 to 0.17 and fight 0.50; with it said on the fight too, the bunker 0.39 to 0.41, fight 0.33 to 0.35, charge 0.09 to 0.14.
127. *mid-72-a, a trident from beyond reach.* Walking on from a diamond it could not get at, the route went under water at y 0, and a drowned threw its trident: 4.5 health a throw through iron, every two seconds, from 19.5 to nothing in seven seconds with no survival step between. A drowned counted as a biter, a threat within eight blocks; a drowned holding a trident is a shooter now, a threat within sixteen or thirty-two once it has hit, and its throw is counted at eight before armour in the fight estimate.
128. *mid-83-d, eight stances in eighteen seconds. Open.* At minute 111, with a diamond pickaxe and obsidian under way, twenty health went to fourteen on a four-block drop among zombies and a skeleton, and from then the encounter was retreat, retreat, retreat, pillar, charge, fight, dig up, seal in eighteen seconds, none carried through, and it was shot at 0.5. A stance is held fifteen seconds only while the kinds of mob in view stay the same and less than six health is lost; here the kinds changed as mobs came into view, and several stances failed and were set aside. How a crowd that grows is met, and what holding a stance through it should mean, is the open question.

129. *Six midgame trials, a quarter of the time on the stairs.* Across the six live midgame trials of 2026-09-26, climbing back to the surface was 110 of 408 minutes, most of it after nights sat out in sealed pockets and night mines, about seven real minutes a night. One bot of the six carried a bed: the bed rung is met once the base's bed is claimed, so after the home is built none was made. The one that carried a brown bed chose seal_here eleven times with it on its back: sleep was offered only where two level cells lay beside the feet, which a staircase and a one-by-two pocket never have, and the shelter question never offered the bed. A second bed to carry is Jev's option on the way to the dragon now, once the base's bed is claimed, said with what it buys (any night passes in seconds, instead of a pocket and the climb out) and what it costs (three wool from sheep or string, three planks), and made the way the first bed is. Where the bed does not fit, a nook is dug for it beside the bot, foot and head, the floor kept and the cell over each cleared: offered at bedtime beside the other night options, in the shelter question (a pocket sealed here and the nook dug from it at bedtime), and from inside a sealed pocket, closed in rock so the pocket stays shut and its wall put back after. Each says that sleep is refused with a monster within about eight blocks sideways and five up or down of the bed, and how many are.
130. *mid-87-d, down a ravine on its own scaffolding.* At minute 106, walking back toward a herd seen earlier, full health, the route went west along a ravine's edge over blocks the pathfinder had placed, and the bot went twenty-six blocks down. The third fall of this kind on a long walk (notes 116, 123). The pathfinder bridges a missing floor cell by placing a block into it and stepping on, unsneaking; over a ravine a slip is the whole fall. It no longer lays a bridge where there is nothing to land on within four blocks below; over a short gap it bridges as before.
131. *The daylight rule, dropped.* The strategy hid a side trip whose walk there and back did not fit in the daylight left, and hid every side trip at night: a rule since Jev sent the bot 240 blocks to a chest with half a minute of day left, told only the distance. Every trip now says its walk against the daylight left, or at night that the whole of it is in the dark and how long till dawn, and the user asked for the choice to be Jev's (2026-09-26: "drop the daylight rule, let Jev choose"). Offered at any hour now; the health, food and threat conditions on side trips stand.
132. *mid-87-e, a smelt held up by two furnaces sixty blocks down.* At minute 26, sealed in a pocket at y 64, Jev chose to make iron here; every smelt first went back for two side furnaces left at y 3 with eleven raw iron each, the walk failed, the failure was passed over, and the smelt returned having done nothing, 579 times in a minute and a half until the audit called the flip with the recovery. Side furnaces out of reach no longer hold up a new batch, and after three failed walks they are let go: the iron is where it was, for a trip that passes.
133. *mid-92-e, a charge at four skeletons and a witch.* At minute 148, at dusk on the way to a village for food, twenty health and full iron: a zombie at nine blocks, four skeletons from sixteen to twenty-three and a witch. The estimate in the state was plain (all of them: eighty-two damage from twenty, risk high), but it was said on the fight option alone, and the charge, listing the five it would run at, said nothing of what they cost; Jev charged, 0.42 to 0.44 replayed, and died in twenty seconds. The charge says the estimate for all the mobs about now; replayed, it fell to 0.11 to 0.13 and the retreat took 0.55 to 0.57.
134. *mid-92-f, a lid opened and closed every two seconds. Partly open.* Seventeen minutes in, on the grass at y 63 with few blocks, the bot was sealed in with a block over its head ("dig in", no mobs, the night), left again ("Moving on."), the lid was dug by the log step as a way to the surface, and it dug in again, every two seconds for a minute, until the audit called it. A night-time "leave" chosen from the pocket is held now as staying up for two minutes, so the next tick does not seal it straight back in; whether that leave was Jev's or the shelter step going for blocks it was short of, the frames do not say, and the step that went for blocks from a pocket that was sealed around the bot is the other suspect.
135. *mid-79-c, under a gravel column at minute 165.* Night-mining iron at y 23, a dig let gravel down onto the head, and it suffocated from nineteen health in twenty seconds. The dig out tried four times a turn and the column refilled the cell after each; between turns the threat checks (a creeper at ten blocks, a skeleton) and the night mine's spare pickaxe had the turn. A block over the head now sends the bot a step aside into open air beside its feet first, as a player steps from under gravel; then the dig goes as often as it takes, twelve times, stopped by nothing but a cancellation; and while the head is still in a block the survival turn ends there, so the dig comes again at once.
136. *mid-83-e, five up on its own dirt for seventy seconds.* At minute 85 Jev stood on a pillar of its own dirt five blocks over the grass, a skeleton nine blocks off, and chose to retreat three times without moving: a route drops three blocks at most, and nothing offered the way down. The pillar's own descent, which digs the top block and drops onto the next, turned back from any hostile near the landing, so it could not run in a fight either. Chosen in a fight it goes now ("come_down"); and on a pillar the retreat says there is no way off it to run by, and the pocket that its walls go up against open air. Replayed, Jev still fought first (0.41 to 0.50) and came down seldom (0.03), and the others fail quickly and are set aside, so the way down is reached where before there was none.
137. *mid-110-i, three hours and no deaths, and no Nether: the arrows.* The first trial to run its whole three hours clean. With everything else done, the ladder's next step was sixteen arrows; here they come only from skeletons (feathers come from chickens, which the bot never hurts, the user's rule), none to two a skeleton, and spent as they are shot: 0 to 4 carried in two hours, thirty-three minutes hunting, the step chosen seventy-three times, and the Nether never on offer, since every step went before it. When every step left before the Nether may wait, going now is Jev's option ("nether_first"), with what going without each costs and the minutes spent; taken, they are set aside for half an hour. The arrows step says where arrows come from here. Replayed at thirty-eight minutes on the arrows: the Nether 0.49 to 0.57, the arrows 0.37 to 0.44.
138. *mid-92-g, in the Nether at minute 27 and shot there at 36.* Ninety seconds into the fortress search, skeletons: a charge, a pillar, and then "keep working" chosen at fifteen health and again at eight, the bot standing at its work while the arrows took it to one. Leaving the mobs be said it ended at the first hit, but a chosen stance was held until six health was gone; a hit ends it now. And the option says what the shooters in sight do meanwhile: about so much damage in the fifteen seconds, from the health the bot has.
139. *mid-92-h, into its own obsidian pool.* Seventeen minutes in, making obsidian at a pool with water poured over it, the bot stepped out of the water it stood in and into the lava below at y 38. The way out of lava was the nearest dry cell, and water did not count as dry: the pour had filled every cell beside it with water, the nearest dry one was seven blocks off through the pool, and the bot burned in four and a half seconds without moving. Out of lava, a cell of water is a way out now, and the best one, since it puts the fire out; out of water, the way out is still dry ground.
140. *mid-72-b, fifty-four minutes "climbing out", twenty-seven of them climbing.* The ascent was the trial's largest line, 53.9 of 180 minutes under ascend_to_surface, but the label outlived the climbs: 18.6 of those minutes were nights waited in a pocket and 6.0 night mining, the step still saying ascend (the minute-36 "ascent" was eight minutes in a pocket at y 50, and the minute-16 one, y 89 down to 75, was the night mine). The climbs themselves were 26.8 minutes, and they were stairs, not the route: no path errors, three stalls, no set-asides. A stair up digs three blocks; with a pickaxe that ran three seconds a stair (257 stairs), by hand sixteen to twenty-four (49 stairs, 13.5 minutes). The by-hand stretches came from the stairs too: three digs a block of height wore out the stone and then the iron pickaxe at y 60 and 65 on the first climb and the iron one at y 58 on the last, no wood carried, and 65 to 73 took 2.9 minutes, with 3.4 more on stairs across to the landing. The other trials' climbs were the same, some 1,080 stairs up with a pickaxe, 3,200 uses. Straight up the column is Jev's other way out now ("climb_out"): one block dug and one put under the feet a block of height, offered where the column to open sky is natural ground with nothing that falls or flows in or beside it and a building block is carried for every step. Each way says its digs, its time with the pickaxes carried, whether they last it, and what it leaves: stairs back down to the mine, or a filled column. It is asked when the climb starts and again when a pickaxe breaks or the column opens or closes; the other climb facts say the straight-up time too. A 24-block shaft took 66 digs by the stairs (for 22 up) and 22 straight up. Replayed on mid-72-b's climbs: straight up 0.77 to 0.92 by hand or with the two worn pickaxes, 0.61 to 0.65 with an iron one too worn for the stairs, 0.56 to 0.61 with a fresh one.
141. *mid-87-f, six minutes at three health waiting to heal.* Hunting at y 24, hurt to three, with sixteen hunger and nothing to eat, the hunt stood "recovering before combat": health comes back only at eighteen hunger or more, so it never came, and below ground hunger did not count as hunger until twelve. A zombie found it at minute 94. Hurt under fourteen health with hunger under eighteen and no food carried counts as hungry now, and the food is gone for.
142. *mid-110-j, burning on the bank with an empty bucket.* Walking toward a herd seen earlier, the route touched a lava pool; the bot was out of it in a second at sixteen health and burned the rest of the way, a health a second, to nothing. The douse pours a carried water bucket at the feet; this bucket was empty, and with none nothing else was tried. Burning with no water carried, water within eight blocks is run into now.
143. *mid-72-c, a bunker against a witch.* Night-mining at y 57 at full health, a witch came within a few blocks and Jev chose to dig into the wall and fight at the doorway; the witch's potions came in at the doorway, twenty health to twelve in four seconds, poison after, and a skeleton ended it at minute 71. Nothing said what a witch's potions do. The bunker and the pillar say it now when one is about: harm that armour does not stop and poison for half a minute, over a pillar and into a doorway, stopped only by a shut pocket.
144. *mid-83-f, in the Nether at minute 102, five legs south from one spot.* The fortress sweep began a leg ninety-six blocks south from (12, 71, -172), the progress watch cut it short, the recovery began the step again, and the sweep, its target gone, began the same leg from the same spot: legs sixteen to twenty, until the audit called the loop at minute 113. The sweep turns after four failed attempts at a leg, and these were never counted. A leg begun again within eight blocks of where the last began turns the sweep now, however the last was cut short.
145. *mid-92-i, knocked off a Nether ledge while it waited to heal.* Forty-seven minutes in the Nether without a fortress, then at seven health on a ledge at y 81 the hunt stood "recovering before combat" while something out of sight burned it a health a second, and at 2.4 a hit put it forty blocks down. The step back from a deadly edge ran only with a mob in view. Hit while recovering, by anything, with a drop within two blocks that would cost half the health or more, the bot goes to ground three blocks from any edge first.
146. *mid-79-d, at a ruined portal and mining none of it.* At minute 128, with a diamond pickaxe and seven obsidian, the step for the last three went to a remembered ruined portal, arrived, and returned; the only obsidian it mined was crust over a pool, and a portal's frame is not crust (air under it, often). "Make obsidian" 673 times in two minutes, until the audit called the loop at 132. At a ruined portal the frame's obsidian is mined where it stands now, and a portal with none left is marked empty so the step goes elsewhere.
147. *mid-79-e, a smelt that failed for its furnace's pick-up.* Four minutes in, fifteen "no route from here to the destination" out of the smelt and the recovery between them, until the audit called the flip. Of the smelt's walks, the one not caught was the last: taking back up the furnace it placed, when travelling; its drop out of reach threw out of a smelt that was done, and the step came round again. The pick-up is taken if it can be now and let go if not; the frames do not show which walk threw, but this is the only one left uncaught with no batch saved.
148. *mid-110-k, a crowd of seven on the way to a village bed. Open (see 128).* At minute 66, on the way at dusk to a village for a second bed, a spider, a zombie, three skeletons and two creepers; Jev chose to retreat, then to pillar with a creeper close, and the blast took the last twelve health. The pillar says a creeper's blast comes before the last block; chosen anyway. With the daylight rule dropped a trip can run into the dark, and the dark is where these crowds are; how a crowd is met is still note 128's open question.
149. *mid-83-g, pillared up into gravel.* At minute 34, climbing out at y 22 with seven health and mobs about, Jev chose to pillar; the pillar digs what is over the head as it rises, gravel among it, and the column came down onto the head: suffocated in four seconds, the dig out no match for it. The pillar, and the climb straight up that goes by it, stop under a falling block and under a block with one over it now.
150. *mid-110-l, the diamond step returning 927 times in four minutes.* At minute 8, on the surface at y 86 with diamonds to mine at y -59 and none in view, the mine step aimed its staircase twenty-four blocks east and down. That staircase was resting after a blocked retreat, so it threw at once, the target was set aside, and the step came back to the same east target at once: 927 returns until the audit called the loop at minute 20. The way down now tries the four headings in turn past any whose staircase rests, and says so when every one does, so the step fails where the recovery can see it.
151. *mid-87-g, spiders and the stance thrash again. Open (see 128).* At minute 97, walking to a herd seen earlier, full health, mobs met at dusk: fight, retreat three times, pillar, fight, retreat, a shaft pocket, pillar, fight, in twenty-five seconds, seventeen health to none. The same thrash as 128; the crowd study is under way.

152. *The crowd at dusk: note 128's open question.* Read from the flight records of the midgame trials of 2026-09-25 and 26. In the fatal encounters most stances were asked because the last one had just failed (five of mid-83-d's eight, five of mid-92-e's seven), not because the kinds in view changed; with three or more mobs about and nothing at arm's length, the retreat failed in 25 of the 39 times it was chosen, the charge in 14 of 17 and the pillar in 7 of 15. The replays showed why the failing ones were taken: the fight carried its cost ("more than the bot has") and the pillar, the pocket and the run carried none. Across all encounters, 135 of the 168 stances asked again for a change of kinds were for a kind gone out of view or one come into view more than eight blocks off. Every stance now says what the mobs cost it over the same fifteen seconds: the building, digging or eating first, with the shield down, then only what still reaches it (two up: the shooters, the climbers and a creeper at the foot), with a creeper's time to go off set against the building's; shooters count from their range (fifteen blocks, a witch ten, one potion in three seconds that a shield does not stop), and the fight says how many can be at arm's length at once. The run's way is looked for before the question, three tenths of a second at most, and said ("a way is found: 14 blocks...", "no way out", "nowhere to run to"); the way found is the one run. Digging straight down where the bot stands and closing the hole over its head (the night's shaft pocket) is a stance of its own, priced the same way at a second and a half a block (the median of 144 shaft pockets in the records); a meal is a stance where it brings health back, and the reflex meal waits while a stance is carried out. A stance is held until it fails, six health is gone, fifteen seconds pass, or a mob it was not chosen against comes within six blocks; one that failed stays off within four blocks of where it failed, whatever the kinds about; the shield raised at each arrow gives way to a stance that moves or builds, and the hurt watchdog to any chosen stance but leaving the mobs be. Replayed, six asks each, on the recorded states: mid-110-k's pillar with the creeper six blocks off 0.45 to 0.19; mid-83-d's charge through three zombies at ten health 0.56 to 0.41, the fight 0.32 to 0.44; mid-92-e's charge at four skeletons and a witch 0.62 to 0.36, and its pillar with a zombie at arm's length 0.68 to 0.32; mid-72-c's bunker against the witch 0.43 to 0.02; and where the run had found no way (mid-110-k twice, mid-92-e), the retreat from 0.39 to 0.54 down to 0.05 to 0.19. mid-83-d's pillar against three zombies and a skeleton rose, 0.66 to 0.93: by the figures two up is the cheap stance there. Offered where the ground allows it (the records do not say; two blocks and 3.4 seconds assumed), digging down was taken first in seven of the twelve asks. In the arena, a new drill of the same crowd (two zombies, a spider, two skeletons and a creeper six to nine blocks off, full health) went one death in eleven runs before (a creeper under the pillar) and one after (a creeper at the edge of the hole), median damage eight to twelve either way: at full health this crowd is fought through, and the drill does not tell the two apart. Open: the pillar fails half the time with nothing at arm's length, and a crowd with shooters in the open at night still costs more than the bot has however it is met where the ground cannot be dug.
153. *The service out, 06:34 to 06:43 UTC.* TypeSafe refused every question ("403: RBAC: access denied") for about nine minutes; every bot ran on its fallbacks or its persist, and mid-87-h's loop was the refusal itself. The six trials that ran through it (mid-79-f, mid-110-m, mid-72-d, which had reached the Nether at minute 88, mid-83-h, mid-87-h, mid-92-j) are void, and six fresh ones began at 06:45 on the code as of the crowd study.
154. *mid-92-k, out of a pocket past the skeletons.* At minute 11, sealed in at dusk at twenty health, Jev chose to leave the pocket twice in ten seconds, told only who was out there and how far; the skeletons shot it from eighteen to nothing in twenty seconds. The leave now says what going out among them is estimated to cost, as the stances do.
155. *mid-87-i, four zombies and a skeleton in a cave, told the cost.* At minute 44, walking through caves at y -15 toward a herd seen earlier, at fifteen health: fighting them all was said at 16.6 damage, more than it had, and Jev fought, then pillared at seven, and was shot down. The facts were there; this is the crowd with no cheap answer (152's open end), met underground on a walk the surface would have carried.
156. *mid-92-l, twenty-five blocks down into the Nether's lava sea. Partly open.* At minute 39, full health, on the fortress sweep at y 53, the bot rose half a block and then fell to the lava at y 28 and burned. A gap jump is refused over lava or a fall of more than five, so it was not one; what put it over the edge the frames do not say. Looked for, a fault of note 123's own: the check for a floor under a diagonal's corner took lava for one, being a liquid; it no longer does.
157. *mid-79-g, a twenty-block pocket begun between two creepers.* At minute 93, night-mining at y 15, full health and iron, two creepers came close and a pocket was begun where the bot stood: twenty blocks to place, about twelve seconds, against a fuse of a second and a half. Blown up from twenty. Which path began it the frames do not say (no question was asked; the night's held shelter plan is the likely one). A pocket is not begun now with a creeper within five blocks and more than two blocks of it to place; the backing off and the dance answer a creeper that near.
158. *mid-87-j, in the Nether at minute 50, a "flip" at 59.* On the fortress sweep, legs fifteen to seventeen from one spot, the sweep turning as note 144 has it, and its tunnel named the step 'tunnel' in turn with 'find_fortress' at every stair: the audit called it a flip between the two. The tunnel is the sweep's phase now, as obsidian's is; the bot was also getting nowhere from that spot (about two blocks either way for twenty seconds), which the progress watch still sees.
159. *mid-92-m, burning in the one dry cell it already stood in.* In the Nether at minute 49, on a fortress leg at y 75 at full health, the body leaned from its cell into a lava flow beside it; the way out of lava was the nearest dry cell, and that was the cell the feet were in, so the step toward it went nowhere and the bot burned from sixteen in four seconds. Out of lava, the cell the feet are in is not a way out now, and cells with no lava beside them come first.
160. *mid-110-n, twenty minutes behind the lid it chose to open.* At minute 116, sealed in a pocket at y -18 at full health in iron with a diamond sword, a zombie and a skeleton about, out of sight: Jev chose to leave the pocket fifteen times, its option stating the mobs and the fight (seven damage from twenty), and each time the leave refused every door within twenty blocks of a mob and waited instead, "Nearby threats still block the shelter exits", so the next tick asked again. A pocket left as Jev chose it goes past the mobs now, by the door farthest from them; leaving for the work alone still waits one out.
161. *mid-92-n, into the pool it was making obsidian of.* At minute 22, at full health, the bot stood in the sheet of water it had poured over a lava pool and swam for a bank a step could be cut into, in a straight line; the line crossed the corner of a cell the pour had not reached, air over lava, and it fell in. Out of lava its one way out was the water on the obsidian a step up, and the climb out of a liquid is refused where the body would rise into liquid: it bobbed against the obsidian for five seconds and burned from sixteen. The swim to a bank crosses only water now, and water a step up is not a way out of lava.
162. *mid-72-e, ten minutes in a pool one block from the bank.* At minute 139, in a one-wide pool with dirt on three sides and a drowned about, Jev chose to climb out onto the bank south ten times; each time the bot rose, pressed against the north bank and sank again, facing north with its keys held for south (the physics, run on the saved terrain, climbs out facing south in a second). What turned it is not in the frames. A held-key move now keeps the heading it was aimed at for as long as its keys are held, and says in the log when something turned it; and a stuck move tried from the same cell before says so to Jev ("tried from here three times already and it did not get there"), with the spell's moves kept across its restarts. Restarted on that code, the bot was put by the server six blocks from where it had thought itself pinned, and swam free: the client's place and the server's may have parted, which a stuck spell should check first next time (the server's own position, beside the flight's).
163. *mid-83-i, out of its pocket into five mobs, at minute 155.* Sealed in underground at full health in iron with a diamond sword, a skeleton and three creepers outside, Jev chose to leave (0.87): its option said fighting them all out there was about nine seconds and 2.5 damage, a sum the creepers were left out of without saying so. Out among them a spider and a second skeleton came in too; a charge at the nearer skeleton lost six health in a second, a retreat found no route, and at two health the bot was shot down while it ate. The leave now says how many creepers the figure leaves out and what one that reaches the bot costs.
164. *mid-110-o, a loop at minute 28.* With a diamond pickaxe, a water bucket and four obsidian, the obsidian step went to a lava pool it had remembered, on a hillside at y 102, and found no lava surface there to pour on from its reach. Arrived, the step returned, and the next pass arrived at it again: twenty-one passes a second for three minutes, the stall rule's detours each coming back to it. A lava pool with no pourable lava in reach once the bot is there is marked spent now, as an emptied ruined portal is, and the step goes looking for lava instead.
165. *mid-87-k, in the Nether at minute 68, on an island in the lava sea.* Its portal came out on a few blocks of netherrack in the open lava sea at y 31, forty blocks and more of lava every way. The fortress sweep bridged forty blocks east at the lava's level, found no shore, came back, and from the island with eighteen blocks left every leg failed, turned and failed again, twelve legs in four minutes. The crossing now waits for two stacks of blocks (cobblestone, deepslate, dirt and the like: 128) before the portal, and the tidy keeps two stacks of cobblestone in the overworld (one in the Nether, where netherrack is the blocks). And a sweep whose legs all fail with under thirty-two blocks carried goes back through the portal for more.
166. *mid-92-o, in the Nether at minute 38, short of blocks where they are the ground.* On the fortress sweep with thirty blocks and two of them netherrack (the tidy keeps netherrack to thirty-two in the Nether), every leg failing, the rule of note 165 sent it for its portal. In the Nether the blocks are underfoot: a stranded sweep mines sixty-four netherrack first, and goes back through the portal only when none is to be had; the tidy keeps ninety-six netherrack in the Nether, and one stack of cobblestone there.
167. *mid-92-o, later: the tidy against the crossing, and soul sand.* Two things the crossing of note 165 would have met: the tidy dropped cobblestone past one stack whenever the pockets crowded, so a bot gathering two stacks for the portal would have gathered, dropped and gathered again; it keeps two now. And out of a soul sand valley the bot carried 105 soul soil and 104 soul sand, four slots of nothing to build with, while it dropped the netherrack it had just mined for the sweep ("leaving 66 netherrack here"); both are surplus now. Burned at y 32 on a fortress leg (from twenty to eight, "something unseen at 0 blocks"), it could not heal at twelve hunger and went back through its portal, which a stall answered first.
168. *mid-92-o, into the lava sea from its soul sand shore.* On a fortress leg at full health the pathfinder took the bot down a slope of soul sand and soul soil to the edge of the lava sea at y 31 and along it; a block and a half off its path, it was in the lava. Out in a second, it burned on from sixteen to seven standing on the shore (no water in the Nether, and the fire runs its course). A cell with lava beside it now costs the pathfinder four more than open ground, so a path along a lava edge is taken only when it is much the shorter way.
169. *mid-92-o, dead at minute 83.* Back from the Nether at eight health and twelve hunger (no healing below eighteen), sealed in a pocket at nightfall, Jev night-mined until the night mine dropped out of the options, and then chose to leave (0.59) over staying (0.08): the stay said "about eight real minutes with nothing gained" and the leave "go back to work in the dark, where mobs spawn", neither the health nor that it was not coming back. Out after food at 8.3, it met a zombie crowd, pillared at 5, and was blown up by a creeper in a shaft pocket. Both now say the health and whether it heals, and the leave how many zombie hits or arrows end it, and whether one creeper's blast does.
170. *mid-83-j, the first fortress: Nether at minute 77, fortress at 78.* At the blaze spawner, nine blazes about it at y 76 and 77, the bot sealed a pocket six blocks below at y 70 and hunted from there; the only approach offered for a target overhead was the cell beneath it at the bot's own level, out of the sword's reach from there and walled off besides, and each hunt ended "No dry combat route to blaze" and the pocket again. A target overhead is now also approached on its own floor, within three blocks of it, by the ground there (the combat movement builds no tower); beneath it first only where the sword reaches up. And the hunt's claim on the kind, staked for five seconds by the hunt's tick, lapsed during the walk to the target, so every fight there ended "Threat nearby: blaze at 5 blocks" five seconds in and the survival layer held the pocket; the fight renews it for as long as it runs.
171. *mid-92-p: Nether at minute 34, fortress at 37, the first blaze rod at about 40, dead at 42.* Hunting blazes the bot went back from its bunker to the fortress, met a wither skeleton at eight blocks at full health in iron with a diamond sword, and went two blocks up on placed blocks: a wither skeleton stands 2.4 and its swing reaches a player two up, so it hit four times while the blocks went down, twenty to one, and the fight chosen at five came too late. The pillar's option told Jev that walkers cannot reach a player two up, the wither skeleton among them. Tall mobs (the wither skeleton, the enderman, the ravager, the iron golem, the warden) are now counted as reaching the pillar and fought in its cost, and the option names them. Also in: a diagnostic line naming the mob that ends a hunt's fight, for mid-83-j's fights under the spawner that ended "Threat nearby: blaze at 4 blocks" with the claim renewed.
172. *Food for the Nether.* The crossing's food gate asked twenty-four points (three steaks). mid-92-p was short of food three minutes after reaching its fortress, and mid-83-j, forty minutes at its spawner, went home hungry through its portal and has been looking for food in the Overworld since; each blaze fought is paid for in hunger, the health coming back from it. The gate asks forty now (five steaks), still with its twenty-minute limit on gathering.
173. *mid-87-l, in the Nether at minute 114, and out, and in.* With two stacks of cobblestone (note 165's gate held) and seventeen pieces of meat, its fortress search set out from beside the Nether portal, and the pathfinder, which took the portal's sheet for air, walked it through: back in the Overworld, the ladder crafted a sword and sent it back through; out again ten seconds later, over and over. The pathfinder avoids the portal now; entering one is the walk up to it and the step in that the crossing already does.
174. *mid-87-l, dead at minute 122.* After note 173's fix, in the Nether at full health on a fortress leg, standing on a ledge at y 74 over the lava sea: a ghast's fireball from thirty blocks took three health and its blast threw the bot off the ledge, forty blocks down into the lava, where it burned from fourteen in four seconds, climbing. With a ghast in sight and a drop beside the feet that ends in lava or costs half the health, the bot steps back to ground three blocks from any edge before anything else.
175. *mid-110-p, suffocated at minute 151; mid-72-f, a witch at minute 141.* mid-110-p climbed out of the ground up a one-wide shaft under a column of gravel; the gravel came down on its head at twelve health. The dig-out dug the block at the head, and in the moment before the next block of the column landed the head's cell was air: the dig stopped there, the work and the food search took their turns, and the next gravel fell on a bot doing something else, four times, twelve health to none in six seconds. The dig goes on now while the column is still coming down (gravel or sand over the head, or a falling block above it). mid-72-f, already at two health with no healing, was finished by a witch on its way home; a slow death with many answers, left to the notes for now.
176. *mid-110-q, blown up at minute 25 at eighteen health in iron.* Fighting a zombie with a creeper eight blocks off (Jev's choice, the creeper said in the option), the stalk it was on stalled in a hole and the stall's answer was to work free, one move at a time: the moves never looked at the mobs, and it was climbing east out of the hole when the creeper went off beside it. Working free now checks for a mob come close before each move and through it, and hands the moment to the survival layer. mid-83-j ran its three hours out with no death and no loop: fortress at 78, twenty minutes of blaze hunting in all, forty-five in the Overworld for food and the climb out, no rod.
177. *mid-92-q, burned a minute after coming out in the Nether, at minute 75.* Its portal came out at y 77 among skeletons, with fire from somewhere (a ghast's fireball, likely): in forty-five seconds Jev chose fight, charge, dig down, fight, charge, retreat and bunker as each failed or a new mob came, twenty health to none, on fire at the end; the portal was five blocks off throughout and not among the stances. In the Nether with a portal within eight blocks, going back through it is now a stance of its own, with the walk to it and the four seconds standing in it said.
178. *mid-92-r, a zombie at minute 50, in iron with a diamond sword.* In a cave at y 8 with a spider, a zombie at arm's length, and a creeper and a skeleton twenty off, from fifteen health: fight, then a shaft pocket dug straight down with the zombie beside the hole (1.4 health a second while the four blocks went), then dig down again at five, then a seal at one. The stances each said what reached the bot while it dug; it died in the shaft. Left in the notes: the crowd-in-a-cave pattern again (notes 128, 163), with the time a dig down takes under a biter the thing to measure next.
179. *mid-87-m, speared by a zombie villager at minute 96.* Underground at y -5 on its way to a lava pool, at eight health: a zombie villager with a spear (26.1's reach weapon) at two blocks, then a spider in the dark; fight, retreat, eat at two, dead. Looked at: it was at twenty health when it dug down to the mobs, and one thrust of the zombie villager's spear took it to 11.4 through full iron, reckoned at three a hit. A mob holding a spear is now reckoned at its thrust (about thirteen before armour) in every estimate Jev is given, and its note says so.
180. *mid-92-s, in lava at minute 21.* Night-mining from a pocket at y 41, gravel on its head and short of air, it worked up one move at a time; with lava in the rock around it, the digs over the head and beside it each said "lava beside it would flow in", and one it had dug let the lava pour down the column onto it, sixteen health to none. Working free no longer offers a dig that lets lava in beside the body; the other moves stay Jev's.
181. *mid-72-g, blown up at minute 121 at full health.* Night-mining from a pocket at y -12, a creeper came to the wall; Jev danced it once from inside, then chose to leave the pocket with it three blocks off behind the rock (the option naming it, heard and not seen). Note 160 had made a chosen leave go past the mobs, and the pocket was opened on the creeper. A leave past the mobs now keeps shut any door within six blocks of a creeper, and waits as before when every door is.
182. *mid-110-r, blown up at minute 106 at full health.* In a cave at y 30, stepping back from an edge, a creeper came down from a ledge nearly eight blocks straight above (at y 37.7, falling, out of sight) and went off as it landed beside the bot, twenty health to none through iron. The creeper out of sight counts within four blocks (note 147); one overhead and falling was not counted until it landed. Left in the notes: the third creeper death in a row not seen coming (notes 181, 176).
183. *mid-83-k, a loop at minute 134: seventeen crafting tables.* Without a pickaxe, up an acacia at y 113 with its pockets full, it made a stone pickaxe from scratch: a log became planks, the planks a table, and the next log vanished; the table carried went too. Not dropped and not placed: each craft left an ingredient in the two-by-two grid of the bot's own inventory, and putting it away had no room to put it in, so it stayed there, out of the item list. The bot crafted table after table from logs it could no longer see until the audit called the flip. The grid is now emptied after room is made for what is in it.
184. *mid-92-u, blown up at minute 24 at full health.* A creeper followed the bot for half a minute through a food hunt and a cook, eight blocks behind; it retreated twice, then at dusk the shelter began, a pocket dug in where it stood, with the creeper still eight off. It went off before the walls were up. A pocket (sealed here or dug in) is not begun now while a creeper in sight would reach it and go off before the last block went in: its walk to lighting distance and its fuse against six tenths of a second a block. The dance and the backing off answer it first.
185. *first-days-201, slain at minute 16 on the first rebuilt server.* The trial servers were rebuilt from scratch (scripts/trials: Java 25, the 26.1.2 server, Fabric with ServerReplay recording Jev) and twelve first-days trials started on fresh worlds. Sealed in a pocket at y 26 in daylight with a zombie, three skeletons, a spider and a creeper about, Jev was asked to stay or leave. riskNow said "low: the mobs about are a fight the bot wins, 2.5 damage": it priced the skeletons behind the wall at nothing, since an unseen shooter shoots nothing. Beside it the leave option said 72.6 damage, more than the bot had. Across the half-minute riskNow swung from 2.5 to 98 as skeletons came in and out of sight through gaps. Jev left at 0.51 to 0.49, went for iron among them, and the stances that followed (charge, dig down, fight, pillar) each failed within two seconds; twenty health to none in eleven. Fighting them all is meeting them all: riskNow now counts every shooter about as in sight, and says how many are in sight beside it. Left in the notes: stances failing in a second or two in a crowd, each taken off the list, until nothing is left that works.
186. *first-days-209, a loop at minute 21: the walk to its own bed.* With the bed placed at home and not yet claimed, the bot was forty blocks off and sixteen down in its night mine. Each time the night mine gave the work back, the bed's step walked to the bed by the plain walk, was told "No path to the goal!" and the detour took over again, twenty-six times, until the audit called the flip. Going home digs when there is no path (note on trial 115), but only the return-home step went that way; the steps done on the site (the bed, the chest, the pond, levelling, the plot, the pen, the restock) each walked there by the plain walk. Now any of them whose walk finds no path goes home the way that digs and tries again; no distance decides it.
187. *first-days-210, rammed to death by a goat at minute 28.* Coming up a staircase onto a mountain with no food carried, a zombie and a fall took it from sixteen health to six and a half, and hunger at sixteen let none of it come back; two more hits left it at 0.7. It spent the next minute at 0.7 looking for food, walked up to a rabbit beside two goats, and one rammed it. Goats ram whoever is near every half-minute to five minutes, and polar bears go for a player near their cubs; to every layer they were animals. Now Jev is told: riskNow names the goats and polar bears within sixteen blocks and what their hit does, and says "high: one hit from the goat would end the bot" when that is so; a hunt target with one within six blocks says so. One that has hurt the bot in the last twenty seconds is attacking it, as in the game, and a threat like any other; there is no rule by health. Left in the notes: a bot at sixteen hunger with no food carried heals nothing, and went up a mountain that way.
188. *first-days-203, shot at minute 32 in a cave by day.* Mining for iron at y 22 with skeletons about, four skeletons found it and every stance was priced above its health. It dug down three blocks under fire, then, with a spider come to arm's length, charged the skeletons at twelve health and went from eleven to one in three seconds. The spider was in no threat list: a spider by day was taken as calm, by the hour. In the game it is the light where the spider stands, twelve or more, that calms it, and a cave at midday is dark. Now a spider by day is calm only in that light (the sky's or a torch's), and one in the dark is a threat as at night.
189. *first-days-212, a loop at minute 33: a dandelion.* Building the pen with the home otherwise done, one fence cell held a dandelion. A placed block replaces ground cover (grass, snow, leaf litter) as the game does, but a flower is not ground cover in the game: a player punches it out first. The place action gave up with "placement obstructed by dandelion" and the step was tried again, seven times, until the audit called the loop. Now anything in the cell that breaks at a touch and stops nothing (a flower, a sapling, a mushroom) is knocked out before placing; a crop is not.
190. *first-days-217, a loop at minute 24: a wood search spent.* Its stone pickaxe worn out, the bot needed wood for sticks and searched for it across plains, beach and sea, some nine hundred blocks, until the search's hundred and twenty-eight legs were spent. Each stall after that was answered by Jev with a look around or an explore, which moved the bot, and the work came back to the same spent search, which failed at once; thirty-two times. Only the "another way" answer turned a search; the others left it spent. The search is now turned (its next leg or heading, the count begun again) whatever the stall's answer, as the comment on it always said.
191. *first-days-206, shot at minute 47 in its own shaft.* At 10.8 health and hunger sixteen, no food carried, it was climbing out of a cave to look for food with a skeleton thirteen blocks off. Jev chose the retreat (told no route was checked yet); the run found one and walked it, and for fourteen seconds the bot bounced up and down its one-wide shaft between y 38 and 45 while the skeleton shot it to 4.8. A walk counted as progressing while it entered cells it had not stood in, and every bounce found one in a three-by-three shaft nine high, so the three-second stall never came and only the fourteen-second timeout ended it. Progress is now getting nearer: a new shortest route left, or a new nearest to the goal. New cells count only for a goal with neither. Also found: Vec3 was used in the walk's own module without being imported there.
192. *first-days-204, a loop at minute 51: a pillar that failed where it stood.* Sealed in for the night at y 118 on a mountain under a structure's oak stairs, the bot was taken as below the surface and sent up; the climb stopped short and working free offered "pillar: rises 1", which failed ("the block did not go under the feet") eleven times from the same cell in two minutes, flipping with the climb until the audit called it. Every move option says when it was tried from that cell before and did not get there; the pillar's failures were recorded as got there, because a pillar rises in its own column and only the column was compared. A move now got there only if it did not fail and, when it rises, reached the height; Jev is told the pillar failed each time.
193. *first-days-211, a loop at minute 48: a staircase backing off.* The best of the batch (full iron by minute 27), it dug a staircase toward iron at y 21, found no stair it could take, backed off to a landing, found none there either, and backed off again; three times in eleven seconds, and the audit called the flip. The staircase was rested only after four retreats with no step between, a count the audit's minute beat. A retreat is for a landing with another way on: when the landing a retreat reached has no step either, or the retreat could not move the bot at all, the staircase is rested at once. No count.
194. *first-days-213, speared at minute 37, and four more deaths in two minutes.* A desert at night: husks, and a camel husk, the 26.1 mount a husk rides with a spear. The bot came up out of its pocket and was lanced from two blocks, 16.3 health to 7.5, then to nothing; respawned, it died to husks three more times and to a creeper. Every layer had the husks at fourteen to nineteen blocks. The server moves a mount and not what rides it, and mineflayer keeps a rider where it mounted, so the lancer stayed where it had climbed on. A rider is now kept at its mount (src/riders.js), and it is a threat where it is. Also seen, for the next note: before the shelter, survival_priority asked the same question every 150 ms, thirty times in five seconds, each answered secure_shelter.
195. *first-days-213 again: the same question thirty times in five seconds.* Before its shelter, the sealing had failed ("Shelter verification failed") and rested three minutes. survival_priority still offered secure_shelter as if it could run; Jev chose it, its run met the resting sealing and returned at once, and the question came again 150 ms later, thirty times. A choice whose way is resting is not a choice: it now rests as long as what it ran into, is left out of the question meanwhile, and the state says why and when it is back (notNow).
196. *first-days-224, a loop at minute 50: grass in a fence cell.* The pen reaches past the levelled footprint (its gate fourteen blocks out, the levelling eight), so its fence ring stands on the ground as it lies, and one fence cell held a bump of grass. Before placing, the site's cells had only the bot's own building blocks cleared out of them (cobblestone, planks, dirt); "placement obstructed by grass_block" seven times. A cell that must be empty now has natural ground dug out of it too, as a player would; chests, beds and other made things are left.
197. *first-days-225, slain at minute 14 at a dungeon.* With an iron sword and no armour, underground at y 41, Jev chose to loot a dungeon fourteen blocks off, told that its spawner keeps making mobs. Four zombies and a skeleton were about it; the bot met them there, every stance priced above its health, and it went from twenty to nothing in eight seconds. The loot option said nothing of what was at the dungeon then. A trip to loot now says what waits at its end: the mobs within sixteen blocks of the place, seen or heard, and what fighting them all there costs with what is carried and worn. Also seen: a stance answered while the bot was at twenty health began only four seconds later, at nine; for a later note.
198. *first-days-221, a loop at minute 53: a llama in the gate.* Building the pen, the gate "did not go where it was placed" two hundred and ninety-one times over half an hour, oak and acacia both; the cell was open air on grass, and a wandering trader's llama stood in it. The game puts no block where a body is, and nothing said a body was there, so every retry and every stall answer looked the same. Placing now looks for a body in the cell first and says whose it is ("a trader llama stands in the cell"), a spectator's excepted; the pen is a rung the stall answers can set aside, now with the reason in front of Jev.
199. *first-days-219, shot at minute 55 at y -51.* In full iron with a diamond sword and a shield, at 9.2 health and hunger fourteen with no food, Jev chose to fight one skeleton, told about 3.8 seconds and 1.3 damage. The skeleton stood on a ledge a block up; the fight could not reach it and stood in its fire for eleven seconds, 9.2 to 2.9, until six health lost handed it back, and the bot died at 0.4 half a minute later. A fixed six health is not what Jev chose on. A fight or a charge now carries the damage and seconds its option stated, and is asked again as soon as it has cost more than that, health or time; the charge says it hands back then. Other stances keep the old rule until their costs are carried as numbers too. Left in the notes: at fourteen hunger with no food the bot heals nothing, again (note 187).
200. *first-days-222, blown up at minute 59.* Out at night gathering wool among spiders, skeletons, endermen and two creepers, the bot fought a spider, and when that ended Jev was asked what to do with a creeper five blocks off: it chose the shelter (0.93), told only that a sealed room is made before the night's mobs. Three seconds later the creeper went off. Every stance says how soon a creeper could go off beside the bot; the shelter did not. It does now: the nearest creeper's distance, the seconds to its blast coming on, and the seconds a shelter takes a block.
201. *Another way into the Nether: a ruined portal.* The user asked that Jev have the game's ways as choices, to play the whole game tree. The only way in was a frame of its own, ten obsidian made from lava and mined with a diamond pickaxe; ruins were only obsidian to quarry with that pickaxe and chests to loot. Now, with no lit portal known and none begun, and a ruined portal remembered within 512 blocks, Jev chooses (portal_method): build a frame, or finish a ruin, each option with its walk, what it needs against what is carried, and whether a diamond pickaxe is needed. At a chosen ruin the frame is fitted to the obsidian standing there (either axis, every slot, the most already in place; crying obsidian in the way only with a diamond pickaxe), its slots and inside are cleared, the missing blocks placed, and it is lit with flint and steel or a fire charge. A ruin that will not make a frame is marked and the question asked again; the frame chosen is not quarried for obsidian. Casting a frame from lava with buckets is next.
202. *mid-215-a, speared at minute 16 by a mounted zombie.* On a mountain at night, a zombie on a zombie horse with a spear (26.1) charged the bot, 11.3 health to 4.4 in one hit. The rider was tracked at its horse (note 194), but Jev chose the retreat four times, 0.72 to 0.89, and was caught each time: a horse is faster than a running player, and the retreat said only that creepers and spiders follow a runner. It now says a rider on a horse or a camel is faster than a running player and a run from it is caught.
203. *mid-211-a, blown up at minute 44.* In a cave at y 30 at night, a skeleton fight took it to 9.2 health with a creeper coming. Jev chose the shelter (told the creeper was nine blocks off and could go off beside the bot in 3.4 seconds), then the shaft pocket, told only "done in seconds"; the creeper was said beside the room and nowhere else. The bot dug down, the creeper followed and went off. Every way to shelter now says how soon a creeper about could go off (the shaft pocket, the night mine, the bed nook, and the pocket here when it is further than seven). First use of the checkpoint ring: the minutes before this death were kept, to be tried again on this code.
204. *mid-205-a, killed at minute 5; the verdict had missed it.* Among zombies, a spider and a creeper at 17.7 health, Jev chose to eat, told about 1.6 seconds and what the mobs would do meanwhile; the eat stance was held, eating again and again for four more seconds while two zombies hit it and the creeper walked up and went off. It died from 0.47 health, and the audit counted damage only in steps of more than half a point, so the verdict said no deaths; a fall to nothing is now always a death, and every promoted world was checked again (all clean). Every priced stance (fight, charge, eat, pillar, seal, dig down) now carries the damage and seconds it was said to cost, and is asked again when it runs ahead of that pace (the damage spread over the seconds, and one blow of the hardest hitter's give) or past its seconds: a stance priced beyond the bot's health is no longer held until it dies. The first retry of a kept death (mid-211-a's last minute, three times on the fixed code) survived three of three.
205. *first-days-232, drowned at minute 41.* Sealed in a pocket at y 29 for the night, crafting an iron pickaxe, water came into the pocket and lifted the bot while it went on crafting, y 29 to 34 in fifteen seconds; it swam up into a column flooded to a granite lid at y 42 and began working free with twelve of twenty breath. Offered: swim up (the head still under water), dig the granite over the head (more stone past it), or put a dirt into the water north or east, "a step up out of the water". That last was false in a column flooded to its lid: a block placed there has water over it. The place move now says a step out only where there is air over the step, and "a block to stand on, with water over it: still under water there" where there is not. Left in the notes: the work went on while water filled the pocket; the pocket breached is not yet said to Jev.
206. *mid-226-a, drowned at minute 5.* Chosen to go home for food, 103 blocks away from y -10, the bot's record fell silent for twenty-three seconds (no frame at all, the event loop held) and came back under water at y -12 with no breath; it drowned in ten seconds more. Nothing in the record says what held the loop. The bot now reports any stall of two seconds or more ("[lag]" in its log, a lag frame in the record) with the step and survival action it was in, so the next one names its code.
207. *mid-205-b, drowned at minute 9.* Working free of water at dusk, the bot sank from y 63 to 60 with its head under, and was asked how to shelter with fourteen of twenty breath; it chose to seal a pocket there and placed block after block for nine seconds. Nothing looked at its air until two were left, too late to swim up: every dig checks breath, and placing did not. Placing now checks breath as digging does, and no shelter is begun with the head under water (a pocket sealed there is full of water); the way up comes first.
208. *mid-202-a, blown up at minute 92; and the first portal.* At y -23 at night among zombies, a skeleton, spiders and a creeper eight blocks off, with a bow and arrows, every stance was priced above its health; the bow was offered only at the skeleton, because only mobs that shoot back were bow targets. A player's answer to a creeper walking up is an arrow or four. The bow is now offered at any mob in clear view at bow range (not an enderman), with the arrows it takes and, for one that walks, how soon it is at the bot or, a creeper, beside it and going off. Also: mid-214-a entered the Nether at minute 45, the first of the rebuilt trials.
209. *mid-236-a, a loop and a death at minute 36.* Its home plot had a cell with nothing beside or under it, the ground taken out (by a blast, by the look of it), and repairing the plot placed dirt into that cell in the air: "no adjacent solid anchor", two hundred and forty times, until a creeper came. A cell over a hole is now filled from the bottom up, as a player fills a crater. The creeper is for the next note. Also: mid-214-a burned at y 16 in the Nether, under the lava sea, the damage read as "something unseen"; two retries from a minute before it lived, so the cause is not yet known.
210. *mid-211-b, burned at minute 66; mid-214-a's lava too.* Climbing straight up out of a hole at y 58, the bot came into lava and burned from seventeen health to four over nine seconds before the survival layer's way out of lava ran; mid-214-a burned at y 16 in the Nether the same way, the damage read as "something unseen". Every dig and walk checks breath many times a second, and the survival layer is handed the step when breath is short; the body in lava was not checked there. It is now, and the survival layer's way out of lava (not itself stopped by the check) comes at once.
211. *mid-230-a, a loop at minute 69; and mid-236-b's falls.* Perched at y 71 by a spruce it wanted a log from, the bot's search found no reachable ground in any direction; Jev was asked a heading every three seconds and chose south each time, and each failed at once. Working free of the terrain (one move at a time) is offered when walks are failing, and "no reachable surveyed ground" was not counted as a walk failing; it is now. Left in the notes: mid-236-b fell sixteen blocks on its way home through a tunnel and then forty-four straight down at the same x and z, the block under it gone; digs beneath the feet are refused, so the cause is not yet known.
212. *mid-226-b, blown up at minute 72.* At y 32 in the dark among zombies, a skeleton, a spider and two creepers, full health, Jev chose the creeper dance with one four blocks off. The skeleton's arrows were in the air, and the shield reflex answered each one before the stance could: two hundred times in three quarters of a second it turned the bot to the arrow and held still, and the creeper walked in and went off, twenty health to thirteen through iron. Every stance was then priced beyond its health, and the second creeper finished it. The shield is not raised at an arrow while a creeper would walk to its lighting distance during the hold (the creeper's reach is its fuse), and the dance, which moves, is not interrupted by the shield, as the retreat and the charge are not.
213. *mid-235-a, fell into lava at minute 62, fourteen minutes into the Nether.* On a fortress leg along a ledge at y 71, the bot stepped into lava beside it (15 of 20 health left) and the way out of lava ran: it holds forward and jump, upright (a crouch sinks in lava), toward the nearest dry cell, until the body is out and on the ground. The cell was on the ledge's edge; the jump carried the bot over it while still in the air, the keys still held, and on over the edge, twenty blocks down into the lava sea. The push now ends as soon as the body is out of the lava and over the cell chosen, and cells with a floor all round come before cells beside a drop.
214. *mid-237-a, killed at minute 22.* In a tunnel at y 28 at night among seven zombies and a skeleton, the bot put up a block to pillar, was knocked off it, and stood a step above the tunnel floor at 13 health. Jev was told it stood "3 blocks up on a pillar", that digging down was the only way off it and that the retreat, the pocket and the charge could not work up there; it chose to come down, and dug under its own feet for four seconds while the zombies took it to four. The height was the stone stacked under the feet, which in a cave goes down for ever. A pillar's height is now the drop to the ground beside it, the least over its open sides; above a route's three-block drop it is still the only way off, and below that the option says a route steps down it too.
215. *mid-230-b, killed by a witch at minute 12.* In a cave at y 23, a witch four to eight blocks off. Jev chose the fight at 18 health, priced at 5.8 seconds and 11.6 damage as if the witch stood to be struck; three runs at it each ended as far off as they began (a witch keeps its distance and throws), and its harming and poison potions took the bot to nothing in twelve seconds. It was asked again twice, told the same price, and told "2 arrows from the witch" would end it. How the last run at a mob went is now said with the fight and the charge when it got no nearer (how long ago, and how far off it ended against where it began), and a witch's blows are potions.
216. *mid-205-c, a loop at minute 83.* In a two-deep hole at y 61 at dusk, the pocket's lid was placed (dig in) and dug out again by the way back to the surface for the oak log it was after, twenty-three times in forty-eight seconds. The stall watcher saw it and struck five times, and nothing changed: dig in is a hold, a wait that is the right thing to be doing, and holds are never set aside. A flip is not a wait: a hold that trades turns with another step is now refused for a minute and a half, so the next answer is taken (never the way out of lava or the other emergencies).
217. *mid-227-a, into the lava sea at minute 110, six minutes into the Nether.* On a fortress leg the bot's tunnel stalled on a ledge at y 77 over the lava sea, and it stood there, not moving, for seven seconds with a magma cube coming down from eighteen blocks over; it was carried a block and a half east with no route or keys held, went over the edge and fell fifty blocks into the lava. Stepping back from a deadly drop came first only with a ghast in sight. It now comes first with any mob in sight among the threats about.
218. *mid-208-a, killed at minute 176.* On a hillside at night among skeletons, creepers and a zombie, Jev chose the creeper dance with one four blocks off; between its steps the reflex that walks the bot back from an edge ran (a mob close and a drop beside), in the creeper's face, and the blast took it from twenty to eleven. A minute of fighting at three health or less followed, and a skeleton finished it. As with the shield (note 212), the edge reflex now gives way to a stance that moves while it holds; the dance and the retreat keep off edges by their own steps (the dance is not danced with a drop within two blocks).
219. *mid-207-a, three hours and no Nether.* Jev chose the arrows 196 times (a bow's arrows come only from skeletons here, none to two each), hunting skeletons for two and a half hours; the Nether-first option was beside it every time. The question's note said "nothing skipped here is skipped for good: every step is done before the Nether", which was false with that option there and told Jev the arrows stood between it and the Nether. The note now says only that nothing skipped is skipped for good, and the question says what the Nether truly waits on: the steps needed before it, and the ones that may wait until after.
220. *mid-236-c, killed by an enderman at minute 38.* With no armour on, fighting a zombie at y 6 by a furnace, an angry enderman came at the bot from twenty blocks. An angry enderman was counted the bot's only within four blocks (so the ones fighting the dragon are not), and this one was on the bot and hitting for seven two seconds after, three hits from 17.8. One that comes three blocks nearer within three seconds is now counted the bot's from there, so the stance question has it while it is still coming. Left in the notes: the trial's first death, to a zombie at minute 14.
221. *mid-214-b, burned to death at minute 45, ten minutes into the Nether.* On a fortress leg at y 51 beside a lava pool, with an enderman about (Jev had chosen the pillar against it), the enderman hit the bot and it was in the lava; two seconds there took it from twenty to twelve, and the fire after, a point a second for twelve seconds, the rest. The step back from the edge counted drops (and drops into lava) as deadly, but not lava level with the feet beside the bot, which a knockback reaches the same. Lava within two blocks at the feet now counts, with a mob close and with any mob in sight.
222. *mid-236-d, killed by a zombie at minute 2.* At nine health in a cave at y 24 with no armour, a zombie eight blocks off: the step back from the edge beside the bot went for a cell a block away, and the way there stalled for three seconds while the zombie walked up and hit it to three; it was fighting only at one. The step back now gives way to the fight when a biter comes to arm's length (not a heavy hitter's toss, which it is for), and rests five seconds so the next look answers the zombie. Left in the notes: the loop spinning at twenty-one passes a second on search_food, just before.
223. *mid-215-b, killed at minute 131, back in the Overworld after reaching the Nether at minute 70.* At night on the surface in full iron among three creepers five to seven blocks off, skeletons and zombies, Jev chose the creeper dance twice (0.85, 0.80), told that a blast takes about half of twenty-two in iron and that it had twenty health; two creepers went off, twenty to six, and the zombies finished it. The dance now says how many creepers there are and how far each is, that it hits one at a time while the others come on, and how many blasts at arm's length (after the armour worn) the health takes.
224. *mid-231-a, blown up at minute 116.* On the surface with creepers about, the stance question went out with one eight blocks off and was answered 3.7 seconds later (most answers come in a twentieth of a second; this one, and a heading question the same minute, took seconds); the bot stood still meanwhile and the creeper went off, twenty to twelve. A second blast at the dance took it to three, and a third creeper, eight blocks off, went off while the next question was still out. A creeper's fuse is a second and a half. While the stance question is out, the bot now backs from a creeper within lighting distance and three, looking at it, as a player steps back while thinking: not with a drop or lava behind, and not past the answer. Left in the notes: why some answers take seconds.
225. *mid-239-a, shot to death at minute 47; and why answers took seconds.* At y 15 with no armour, a skeleton twelve blocks off, the bot's event loop was held 3.3 seconds as an arrow took it from thirteen to eight, and no stance was answered in the nine seconds before it died. Answers to the stance question had taken 3.7 seconds in mid-231-a (note 224) though the service answered in a fifth of a second (the median over 197 of them; none over one second): the time was the bot's own event loop, held two to four seconds at a time. Twenty trial servers and twenty bots, and the replay client idling at one and a half cores, held this sixteen-core machine at a load of sixteen. The replay client is closed, and trials are cut to fourteen as they finish (25595 is stopped).
226. *mid-226-c, blown up at minute 22.* At night on the surface hunting spiders for string, no shelter reachable, the bot began a pocket where it stood and walled for five seconds; a creeper walked up while it did, and one blast, with only a chestplate on, took it from twenty to nothing. Whether a creeper would reach the pocket and go off before its last block was checked when the walling began, not after. It is checked before every block now; the walling stops and the mobs are answered when one would.
227. *mid-208-b, blown up at minute 17, not yet understood.* In a cave at y 16 in full iron, five creepers within twenty blocks and three zombies, fighting a zombie at arm's length at 14.9 health, one blast took all of it. The creepers in sight at the last question were fifteen to twenty blocks off; the one that went off was not among the threats named, so most likely it came round a corner out of sight. Twenty seconds before, with a creeper four blocks off, Jev had chosen to keep working (0.38), told that one at three blocks is already lighting. Left in the notes: a creeper out of sight between four and eight blocks is not named as a threat.
228. *mid-214-c, blown up at minute 12.* In a cave at y 16 after a skeleton fight, at 18.6 health and eighteen food, the threat check stopped the step with a creeper eight blocks off and in sight, and the survival layer's first act was a meal: it stood eating three and a half seconds, no stance asked, and one blast took all of it with only a chestplate on. The meal waited only for a mob within five blocks. A creeper now counts as close to it out to its lighting distance plus what it walks in twice a meal's time (about twelve blocks); starving, the bot still eats.
229. *mid-218-a, three hours and no Nether.* Jev chose the ruined portal 142 blocks off (portal_method ruin_0); three of its ten obsidian stood, and the seven missing were to be made from lava and water and mined with a diamond pickaxe, the same work as a frame of its own. The rest of the run went to that obsidian, to iron (2,210 mining steps at iron ore) and to a hunt for Nether food that stood at 37 of 40 points for twenty-four passes. The option for a ruin said what it needed against what was carried, but a ruin short of obsidian without diamonds is still the diamond route. Casting the frame's missing blocks in place from lava and water, which needs no pickaxe at all, is the Nether route still missing from the ladder, and is next.
230. *The ruined-portal option told the truth about its obsidian.* After mid-218-a (note 229): the option for a ruin now says how many of the frame's ten are missing (from what was seen when it was found), that they are obsidian, how many are carried, and, short, that the rest are made from lava with a diamond pickaxe (carried or not, and the diamonds for one) unless the ruin's chest holds them.
231. *mid-237-b, killed by a witch at minute 44.* On a mountain at y 105 a witch poisoned the bot through a retreat; poisoned at 12 health with the witch following ten blocks off, the survival layer ate, standing still three seconds, and a harming potion took four; the poison did the rest. The meal waited only for a mob within five blocks. It now waits for a mob that throws or shoots while it is in that mob's range and sight (a witch's ten blocks, a skeleton's fifteen, a ghast's forty); starving, the bot still eats.
232. *mid-220-a and mid-218-a, three hours each and no Nether.* Both stood at the crossing short of the Nether food reserve, forty points: mid-220-a at thirty-nine for forty-four passes of hunting, mid-218-a at thirty-seven for twenty-five; the gate let the crossing go only after twenty minutes with no more food found. A reserve the bot cannot see past is a hidden rule. Short of it with some food carried, Jev is now asked (nether_food) whether to cross with what is carried or gather more, told what is carried, what the reserve is for, what is in the home chest and how long the gathering has run; held ten minutes once chosen. With nothing carried the bot still gathers.
233. *mid-235-b, a loop at minute 57.* At dusk in a two-deep hole, the work step's climb to the surface for an oak log dug out the lid of the shelter the survival layer had just sealed, and the survival layer sealed it again, while the work traded work_free (dig up toward the sky) and the log's mining: the audit called the loop. The stall watcher struck eight times on the return to the surface, and nothing changed: that name is set by the work step's climb, where no refusal is looked at, and the shelter on the other side was a hold. Both sides of a survival flip now rest for a minute and a half, so the sealed shelter is refused too and the next answer is taken.
234. *A frame cast in place, the third way into the Nether.* After mid-218-a (note 229), which spent its three hours on the diamond route to finish a ruin, the portal question offers a frame of its own cast in place, and is asked whether or not a ruin is known. Each missing block is a lava bucket poured into its slot, walled round first with temporary blocks (lava runs down and sideways, never up), then water poured into the cell above it or beside that and taken back; the lava source turns to obsidian where it stands, and no pickaxe touches it. The bot stands beside the frame, across it, where both pours can be seen into from anywhere in the block it stops in. The option says what it needs against what is carried (a water bucket, buckets at three iron each, about forty-four ordinary blocks for the walls, flint and steel or a fire charge), that each block is one bucket so the trips to lava are the blocks over the buckets carried, and how far the nearest known lava is. Lava buckets are filled from dry ground at a pool's edge, a block above the lava. Not yet seen in a trial.
235. *mid-215-c, a loop at minute 36: "invalid operation".* Crafting a stone pickaxe at a table failed twenty times with the window's own refusal of a click, "invalid operation", and nothing else said; mid-220-a had it twenty-six times on birch planks, and four other trials a few times each. A refused click is now logged with the slot, the button and mode, the window and its size, what is on the cursor, and where it came from, so the next one names its cause.
236. *mid-220-b, a loop at minute 31.* On a beach at y 63, the bot took itself for underground and its climb to the surface stood still at a landing three blocks off, forty-five seconds at a time, eight times; each time Jev was asked for a detour and chose "keep at it another way" (sixteen times in all), told only what that would do.
